# Codex verification brief — structured answers (commit aa5655a)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/s1/`. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## Context

Six rounds of review of a free-prose claim checker (`src/claims.py`; reports in `audit_evidence_claims/`)
never converged, so the design changed. With a language model, the model no longer writes the answer: it
returns `{"claims": [...]}` where each claim is a fixed-kind JSON object citing an evidence edge id.
`src/structured.py` (`Verifier`, `render`) checks each claim exactly against the SQLite graph and the
program writes the answer from accepted claims only, using graph labels and values. `src/query.py`
(`answer` with `use_llm=True`, `_llm_claims`, `verify_structured`) wires it in; `src/app.py`
(`/api/structured`) and `web/try.html` box 3 expose it. `claims.py` stays as an auxiliary screen (box 4)
and is documented as fallible (README known defect 13) — it is not under review here.
Tests: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (300); `tests/test_structured.py` covers this.

## The guarantee claimed (module docstring, README "Asking it things", dashboard section 06)

An accepted claim is supported by the graph: correct kind and exact keys (no free-text field; unknown keys
reject), cited edge among those retrieved for the question and live, relation and both endpoints equal to
the named entities (names must resolve, whole, to exactly those node ids), and every value equal to what the
graph stores (action type, sponsor trial count, trial phase set and status, drug max stage, trials in graph).
The rendered answer contains nothing typed by the model. It does NOT claim the chosen claims answer the
question well or completely.

## What to verify (reproduce everything; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **False acceptance.** Any claim the graph does not support that is accepted, on the demo questions in
   `src/query.py:main` or any other question. Try: name resolution (aliases, salts vs parents, ambiguous
   names, companies with several ids via `also_matching_ids`, parent/subsidiary, case/whitespace/Unicode,
   very long names, names that link partially), id vs name mixing, evidence ids from a different question,
   PROFILE pseudo-edges, retired edges, relation/edge mismatches, value normalization in `_norm`
   (can two different stored codes normalize to the same string? e.g. phase sets, statuses, stages,
   action types like "ANTISENSE INHIBITOR" vs "INHIBITOR"), type confusion (bool/int/float/str/None/lists of
   mixed types, duplicate JSON keys, NaN/Infinity), nested or oversized payloads, more than 50 claims.
B. **Text leakage.** Any way for model-typed text to reach the rendered answer or the user-facing page
   (render uses labels from the DB; check every field and the page's display of rejected claims for HTML
   injection in `web/try.html`).
C. **Rendering faithfulness.** Does each rendered line say exactly what the accepted facts say (e.g. the
   sponsorship line, phase lists, empty phases, action wording)?
D. **The model path.** `query.answer(use_llm=True)` and `_llm_claims`: prompt, JSON extraction from the
   reply (`re.search(r"\{.*\}", raw, re.S)`), behaviour on malformed or empty replies, the checks dict.
E. **Usefulness.** Can faithful claims an LLM would produce from the evidence shown be expressed and
   accepted for each demo question (relations, trials' phase/status, sponsor counts, drug stage/count)?
   Note claim types the schema cannot express.
F. **Docs.** README "Asking it things" and item 13, README.ko, `structured.py` docstring, dashboard
   section 06 (`web/template.html`), try page box 3/4 text: do they claim more than the code does?

## Approval bar

No known way to get a claim the graph does not support accepted, or model-typed text into the answer;
faithful claims for the demo questions can be expressed and are accepted; docs do not overclaim.
Findings that are evidence-consistent or purely about completeness are NOTES.

Report: BLOCKERS, SHOULD FIX, NOTES, each with exact input and observed output. Then the verdict line.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

"""Structured answers: claims checked exactly against the graph, prose written from them.

Reading free prose for unsupported claims is a pattern match over sentence shapes,
and six adversarial rounds kept finding shapes it misread (see claims.py and README
known defect 13). This module removes the problem instead of chasing it: a language
model does not write the answer, it fills in claims, and the program writes the
answer from the claims that check out.

A claim is a small JSON object of a fixed kind with fixed keys:

  {"kind": "relation", "relation": "ACTS_ON", "subject": "osimertinib",
   "object": "EGFR", "evidence": "<edge id>", "action": "INHIBITOR"}          (action optional)
  {"kind": "relation", "relation": "EVALUATES", "subject": "NCT03909334",
   "object": "osimertinib", "evidence": "<edge id>"}
  {"kind": "relation", "relation": "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", "subject": "AstraZeneca",
   "object": "osimertinib", "evidence": "<edge id>", "trial_count": 14}       (trial_count optional)
  {"kind": "trial", "trial": "NCT03909334", "evidence": "<edge id>",
   "phase": ["PHASE2"], "status": "ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING"}                    (phase, status optional; one needed)
  {"kind": "drug", "drug": "osimertinib", "evidence": "<edge id>",
   "max_stage": "APPROVAL", "trials_in_graph": 118}                           (one of the two needed)

A claim is accepted only when all of these hold:

  * its kind and keys are exactly as above (an unknown key rejects the claim, so no
    free text can ride along) and every value has the expected type;
  * the cited evidence id is one of the edges retrieved for this question, and that
    edge is live in the graph;
  * the edge's relation is the claimed relation, and its two ends are the entities
    the claim names (a name must resolve, as a whole, to exactly that node id);
  * every value equals what the graph stores: the edge's action type, the sponsor's
    trial count, the trial's phase set and status, the drug's maximum stage, and the
    number of live trials evaluating the drug.

The answer shown to the reader is written by `render` from accepted claims only, using
the graph's own labels and values, never the claim's strings. A claim that does not
check out is reported with the reason and left out of the answer.

What this does not do: it cannot tell whether the claims chosen answer the question
well, or whether something important was left out. It guarantees that what is said is
in the graph, not that everything in the graph that matters was said.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
import sqlite3

RELATIONS = {"ACTS_ON": ("Drug", "Target"), "EVALUATES": ("Trial", "Drug"),
             "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF": ("Company", "Drug")}
KEYS = {
    "relation": ({"kind", "relation", "subject", "object", "evidence"},
                 {"action", "trial_count"}),
    "trial": ({"kind", "trial", "evidence"}, {"phase", "status"}),
    "drug": ({"kind", "drug", "evidence"}, {"max_stage", "trials_in_graph"}),
}
RE_NCT = re.compile(r"NCT\d{8}")


def _norm(v: str) -> str:
    """Spelling-insensitive form of a stored code: "phase 2" = "PHASE2" = "Phase-2"."""
    return re.sub(r"[\s\-_/]+", "", v.strip().upper())


def _flat(s: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", " ", s.lower()).strip()


class Verifier:
    def __init__(self, kg):
        self.kg = kg

    # -- entity names --------------------------------------------------------- #
    def resolve(self, name, want: str) -> tuple[set[str], str | None]:
        """Node ids a name stands for, as a whole, among nodes of type `want`."""
        if not isinstance(name, str) or not name.strip() or len(name) > 120:
            return set(), "the name must be a short non-empty string"
        name = name.strip()
        row = self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT id, type FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                   (name,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            return ({row["id"]}, None) if row["type"] == want else (
                set(), f"{name} is a {row['type'].lower()}, not a {want.lower()}")
        if want == "Trial":
            return set(), "a trial is named by its registry id (NCT followed by 8 digits)"
        ents = [e for e in self.kg.link_entities(name) if e.get("type") not in ("Disease",)]
        whole = len(ents) == 1 and _flat(ents[0].get("matched_text") or "") == _flat(name)
        if whole and ents[0].get("type") != want:
            return set(), f"{name} is a {ents[0]['type'].lower()}, not a {want.lower()}"
        if not whole:
            return set(), f"'{name}' does not name exactly one {want.lower()} on its own"
        e = ents[0]
        return {e["id"], *e.get("also_matching_ids", [])}, None

    # -- one claim ------------------------------------------------------------ #
    def check(self, claim, retrieved: set[str]) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None]:
        """Return (facts to render, None) or (None, reason)."""
        if not isinstance(claim, dict):
            return None, "a claim must be a JSON object"
        kind = claim.get("kind")
        if kind not in KEYS:
            return None, f"unknown kind {kind!r}; use relation, trial or drug"
        need, may = KEYS[kind]
        keys = set(claim)
        if need - keys:
            return None, f"missing {sorted(need - keys)}"
        if keys - need - may:
            return None, f"unknown keys {sorted(keys - need - may)} (no free text is accepted)"
        ev = claim["evidence"]
        if not isinstance(ev, str) or ev not in retrieved:
            return None, "the cited evidence is not among the edges retrieved for this question"
        edge = self.kg.conn.execute(
            "SELECT * FROM edge WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL", (ev,)).fetchone()
        if kind == "drug" and edge is None and ev.startswith("profile:"):
            edge = {"id": ev, "rel": "PROFILE", "src": ev.split(":", 1)[1], "dst": None,
                    "props": "{}"}
        if edge is None:
            return None, "the cited evidence is not a live edge of the graph"
        return getattr(self, "_" + kind)(claim, edge)

    def _relation(self, c, edge):
        rel = c["relation"]
        if rel not in RELATIONS:
            return None, f"unknown relation {rel!r}"
        if edge["rel"] != rel:
            return None, f"the cited edge is {edge['rel']}, not {rel}"
        st, ot = RELATIONS[rel]
        s_ids, why = self.resolve(c["subject"], st)
        if why:
            return None, why
        o_ids, why = self.resolve(c["object"], ot)
        if why:
            return None, why
        if edge["src"] not in s_ids or edge["dst"] not in o_ids:
            return None, "the cited edge does not join these two entities in this direction"
        props = json.loads(edge["props"] or "{}")
        facts = {"kind": "relation", "relation": rel, "edge": edge["id"],
                 "subject": edge["src"], "object": edge["dst"]}
        if "action" in c:
            if rel != "ACTS_ON" or not isinstance(c["action"], str):
                return None, "an action belongs to an ACTS_ON claim, as a string"
            if _norm(c["action"]) != _norm(props.get("action_type") or ""):
                return None, (f"the graph gives the action as {props.get('action_type') or 'none'}, "
                              f"not {c['action']}")
            facts["action"] = props["action_type"]
        if "trial_count" in c:
            n = c["trial_count"]
            if rel != "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF" or type(n) is not int:
                return None, "a trial_count belongs to a SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF claim, as a whole number"
            if n != props.get("evidence_trials"):
                return None, f"the graph gives {props.get('evidence_trials')} trials, not {n}"
            facts["trial_count"] = n
        return facts, None

    def _trial(self, c, edge):
        if edge["rel"] != "EVALUATES":
            return None, "a trial's phase or status is cited from an EVALUATES edge of that trial"
        ids, why = self.resolve(c["trial"], "Trial")
        if why:
            return None, why
        if edge["src"] not in ids:
            return None, "the cited edge belongs to a different trial"
        if "phase" not in c and "status" not in c:
            return None, "a trial claim states a phase, a status or both"
        node = self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT props FROM node WHERE id=?", (edge["src"],)).fetchone()
        props = json.loads(node["props"] or "{}")
        facts = {"kind": "trial", "edge": edge["id"], "trial": edge["src"]}
        if "phase" in c:
            ph = c["phase"]
            if not isinstance(ph, list) or not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in ph):
                return None, "phase is a list of strings, e.g. [\"PHASE2\"]"
            if sorted(_norm(x) for x in ph) != sorted(_norm(x) for x in props.get("phases") or []):
                return None, f"the graph gives the phase as {props.get('phases')}, not {ph}"
            facts["phase"] = props.get("phases")
        if "status" in c:
            if not isinstance(c["status"], str) or _norm(c["status"]) != _norm(props.get("status") or ""):
                return None, f"the graph gives the status as {props.get('status')}, not {c['status']}"
            facts["status"] = props.get("status")
        return facts, None

    def _drug(self, c, edge):
        ids, why = self.resolve(c["drug"], "Drug")
        if why:
            return None, why
        if edge["rel"] not in ("ACTS_ON", "PROFILE") or edge["src"] not in ids:
            return None, "a drug's stage or trial count is cited from that drug's own statement"
        if "max_stage" not in c and "trials_in_graph" not in c:
            return None, "a drug claim states max_stage, trials_in_graph or both"
        node = self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT props FROM node WHERE id=?", (edge["src"],)).fetchone()
        props = json.loads(node["props"] or "{}")
        facts = {"kind": "drug", "edge": edge["id"], "drug": edge["src"]}
        if "max_stage" in c:
            if not isinstance(c["max_stage"], str) or \
                    _norm(c["max_stage"]) != _norm(props.get("max_phase_overall") or ""):
                return None, (f"the graph gives the maximum stage as "
                              f"{props.get('max_phase_overall')}, not {c['max_stage']}")
            facts["max_stage"] = props.get("max_phase_overall")
        if "trials_in_graph" in c:
            n = c["trials_in_graph"]
            real = self.kg.conn.execute(
                "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                (edge["src"],)).fetchone()[0]
            if type(n) is not int or n != real:
                return None, f"the graph gives {real} trials evaluating this drug, not {n}"
            facts["trials_in_graph"] = real
        return facts, None

    # -- a whole answer -------------------------------------------------------- #
    def verify(self, payload, evidence) -> dict:
        """`payload` is the model's JSON (a string or already parsed)."""
        if isinstance(payload, str):
            try:
                payload = json.loads(payload)
            except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
                return {"verified": False, "accepted": [], "text": "",
                        "rejected": [{"claim": None, "why": f"not valid JSON: {e.msg}"}]}
        claims = payload.get("claims") if isinstance(payload, dict) else None
        if not isinstance(claims, list) or not claims or set(payload) != {"claims"}:
            return {"verified": False, "accepted": [], "text": "",
                    "rejected": [{"claim": None,
                                  "why": 'the answer must be {"claims": [...]} and nothing else'}]}
        retrieved = {e.edge_id for e in evidence}
        accepted, rejected = [], []
        for c in claims[:50]:
            facts, why = self.check(c, retrieved)
            if why:
                rejected.append({"claim": c, "why": why})
            else:
                accepted.append(facts)
        if len(claims) > 50:
            rejected.append({"claim": None, "why": "more than 50 claims; the rest were not read"})
        return {"verified": not rejected, "accepted": accepted, "rejected": rejected,
                "text": render(accepted, self.kg.conn)}


def render(facts: list[dict], conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> str:
    """Sentences from accepted facts, in the graph's own words."""
    def label(i):
        r = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=?", (i,)).fetchone()
        return r[0] if r else i

    lines = []
    for f in facts:
        if f["kind"] == "relation" and f["relation"] == "ACTS_ON":
            act = f" ({f['action'].lower()})" if f.get("action") else ""
            lines.append(f"{label(f['subject'])} acts on {label(f['object'])}{act}")
        elif f["kind"] == "relation" and f["relation"] == "EVALUATES":
            lines.append(f"{label(f['subject'])} evaluates {label(f['object'])}")
        elif f["kind"] == "relation":
            n = f" {f['trial_count']}" if "trial_count" in f else ""
            lines.append(f"{label(f['subject'])} is lead sponsor of{n} trial(s) evaluating "
                         f"{label(f['object'])} - a sponsorship, not evidence of developing "
                         "the drug")
        elif f["kind"] == "trial":
            bits = []
            if "phase" in f:
                bits.append("phase " + ", ".join(f["phase"]) if f["phase"] else "no phase given")
            if "status" in f:
                bits.append("status " + f["status"])
            lines.append(f"{label(f['trial'])}: " + "; ".join(bits))
        else:
            bits = []
            if "max_stage" in f:
                bits.append(f"maximum clinical stage {f['max_stage']}")
            if "trials_in_graph" in f:
                bits.append(f"{f['trials_in_graph']} NSCLC trial(s) in this graph")
            lines.append(f"{label(f['drug'])}: " + "; ".join(bits))
        lines[-1] += f" [{f['edge']}]"
    return "\n".join(f"- {x}" for x in lines)



 succeeded in 359ms:
"""Grounded question answering over the graph.

The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.

Three properties the answering layer holds to:

* **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
  each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
* **The graph answers, not the model.** The language model, when a key is
  present, is given the retrieved evidence and nothing else; with no key the
  same evidence is rendered directly. The facts are identical either way, so the
  model can only change the prose.
* **The answer is checked against its own evidence.** Entities and numbers in
  the generated text are verified against the retrieved set, and anything
  unsupported is reported rather than shipped. An unverifiable answer is worse
  than a short one.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T

DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# evidence
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass
class Evidence:
    statement: str
    edge_id: str
    relation: str
    source: str
    source_record: str | None
    fetched_at: str | None
    confidence: float
    tier: str | None = None
    url: str | None = None
    detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)

    def citation(self) -> str:
        base = self.source_record or self.source
        return f"[{base}]"


@dataclass
class Answer:
    question: str
    intent: str
    entities: list[dict]
    text: str
    evidence: list[Evidence]
    generated_by: str
    checks: dict

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "question": self.question,
            "intent": self.intent,
            "entities": self.entities,
            "answer": self.text,
            "generated_by": self.generated_by,
            "checks": self.checks,
            "evidence": [
                {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
                 "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
                 "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
                 "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
                for e in self.evidence
            ],
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# engine
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
class OncoKG:
    def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
                 resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
        self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
        self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
        self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
        self.disease = resolved["disease"]
        self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
                        if t.get("symbol")}
        # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
        # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
        self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
        for row in self.conn.execute(
                "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
            self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
            self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
        # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
        for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
            if same in self.companies:
                self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
        self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}

    # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
    def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
        found: list[dict] = []
        seen: set = set()
        tokens = [t.strip(" ?.,!;:") for t in re.split(r"[\s,]+", question)
                  if t.strip(" ?.,!;:")]

        # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too. Linking straight
        # against the index let "Mvasi" or "liposomal doxorubicin" answer as
        # the originator molecule. A token naming a distinct product is
        # refused; a qualifier ("liposomal", "biosimilar") takes its
        # neighbours with it.
        refused: set[int] = set()
        for i, tok in enumerate(tokens):
            if T.is_distinct_product(tok) or T.is_radiotracer(tok):
                refused.add(i)
                if not T.canonical_key(tok) in self.dic["index"]:
                    refused.update({i - 1, i + 1})
        refused = {x for x in refused if 0 <= x < len(tokens)}
        run: list[int] = []
        for i in sorted(refused) + [None]:
            if run and (i is None or i != run[-1] + 1):
                span = " ".join(tokens[run[0]:run[-1] + 1])
                found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": span,
                              "matched_text": span,
                              "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
                                     "the originator molecule"})
                run = []
            if i is not None:
                run.append(i)

        for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
            for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
                if refused & set(range(i, i + size)):
                    continue
                window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
                if len(window) < 3:
                    continue
                key = T.canonical_key(window)

                hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
                # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
                # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
                # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
                if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
                    hits = None
                if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
                    did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
                    if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Drug", did))
                        found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
                                      "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
                                      "matched_text": window,
                                      "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
                    continue

                if window.lower() in self.targets:
                    t = self.targets[window.lower()]
                    if ("Target", t["target_id"]) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Target", t["target_id"]))
                        found.append({"type": "Target", "id": t["target_id"],
                                      "label": t["symbol"], "matched_text": window})
                    continue

                if key in self.companies:
                    cid = self.companies[key][0]
                    if ("Company", cid) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Company", cid))
                        row = self.conn.execute(
                            "SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=?", (cid,)).fetchone()
                        found.append({"type": "Company", "id": cid,
                                      "label": row["label"], "matched_text": window,
                                      "also_matching_ids": self.companies[key][1:]})
                    continue

                if key in self.disease["index"]:
                    if ("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]) not in seen:
                        seen.add(("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]))
                        found.append({"type": "Disease",
                                      "id": self.disease["disease_id"],
                                      "label": self.disease["name"],
                                      "matched_text": window})
        return found

    def org_family(self, company_id: str) -> list[str]:
        """The organisation plus any companies a curated parent edge links to it."""
        ids = {company_id}
        for row in self.conn.execute(
                "SELECT dst FROM edge WHERE rel='PARENT_OF' AND src=?"
                " AND retired_at IS NULL", (company_id,)):
            ids.add(row["dst"])
        return sorted(ids)

    # -- intent ------------------------------------------------------------ #
    @staticmethod
    def classify(question: str, entities: list[dict]) -> str:
        q = question.lower()
        types = {e["type"] for e in entities}
        if re.search(r"\b(who|which compan|which firm|sponsor|develop|partner)", q):
            return "companies_for_drug" if "Drug" in types else "companies_for_target"
        if re.search(r"\b(trials?|stud(y|ies)|nct)\b", q):
            return "trials_for_entity"
        if re.search(r"\b(target|mechanism|acts? on|moa)\b", q) and "Drug" in types:
            return "targets_of_drug"
        if "Target" in types and re.search(r"\b(drug|inhibitor|agent|candidate)", q):
            return "drugs_for_target"
        if "Company" in types:
            return "company_portfolio"
        if "Drug" in types:
            return "drug_profile"
        if "Target" in types:
            return "drugs_for_target"
        return "unsupported"

    # -- retrieval --------------------------------------------------------- #
    def _rows(self, sql: str, params: tuple) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
        return list(self.conn.execute(sql, params))

    def retrieve(self, intent: str, entities: list[dict], limit: int = 12):
        ev: list[Evidence] = []
        drug = next((e for e in entities if e["type"] == "Drug"), None)
        target = next((e for e in entities if e["type"] == "Target"), None)
        company = next((e for e in entities if e["type"] == "Company"), None)

        if intent == "companies_for_drug" and drug:
            for r in self._rows("""
                SELECT e.id, e.src, e.confidence, e.props, n.label AS company
                FROM edge e JOIN node n ON n.id = e.src
                WHERE e.rel='SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF' AND e.dst=? AND e.retired_at IS NULL
                ORDER BY json_extract(e.props,'$.evidence_trials') DESC LIMIT ?
            """, (drug["id"], limit)):
                p = json.loads(r["props"])
                ev.append(Evidence(
                    # sponsorship only; the sponsor class is stated so a reader
                    # can tell an originator from a cooperative group
                    statement=f"{r['company']} ({(p.get('sponsor_class') or 'unknown class').lower()} "
                              f"sponsor) is lead sponsor of "
                              f"{p['evidence_trials']} trial(s) evaluating {drug['label']}"
                              " - a sponsorship, not evidence of developing the drug",
                    edge_id=r["id"], relation="SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", source="derived",
                    source_record=", ".join(p.get("evidence_sample", [])[:3]),
                    fetched_at=None, confidence=r["confidence"],
                    detail={"derived_rule": p.get("rule"),
                            "sponsor_class": p.get("sponsor_class"),
                            "evidence_trials": p["evidence_trials"],
                            "example_trials": p.get("evidence_sample", [])[:3]}))

        elif intent in ("trials_for_entity",) and drug:
            for r in self._rows("""
                SELECT e.id, e.src, e.confidence, e.tier, e.props, e.source_record,
                       e.source_fetched_at, n.props AS tprops, n.label AS nct
                FROM edge e JOIN node n ON n.id = e.src
                WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.dst=? AND e.retired_at IS NULL
                ORDER BY e.confidence DESC LIMIT ?
            """, (drug["id"], limit)):
                tp = json.loads(r["tprops"])
                p = json.loads(r["props"])
                ev.append(Evidence(
                    statement=f"{r['nct']} ({', '.join(tp.get('phases') or []) or 'phase n/a'}, "
                              f"{tp.get('status')}) evaluates {drug['label']}",
                    edge_id=r["id"], relation="EVALUATES", source="clinicaltrials.gov",
                    source_record=r["source_record"], fetched_at=r["source_fetched_at"],
                    confidence=r["confidence"], tier=r["tier"],
                    url=TRIAL_URL.format(r["nct"]),
                    detail={"stated_intervention": p.get("stated_intervention"),
                            "title": tp.get("title"), "status": tp.get("status"),
                            "phases": tp.get("phases")}))

        elif intent == "targets_of_drug" and drug:
            for r in self._rows("""
                SELECT e.id, e.confidence, e.props, e.source_record,
                       e.source_fetched_at, n.label AS symbol, n.props AS tprops
                FROM edge e JOIN node n ON n.id = e.dst
                WHERE e.rel='ACTS_ON' AND e.src=? AND e.retired_at IS NULL LIMIT ?
            """, (drug["id"], limit)):
                p = json.loads(r["props"])
                ev.append(Evidence(
                    statement=f"{drug['label']} acts on {r['symbol']}"
                              + (f" ({p['action_type'].lower()})" if p.get("action_type") else ""),
                    edge_id=r["id"], relation="ACTS_ON", source="Open Targets / ChEMBL",
                    source_record=r["source_record"], fetched_at=r["source_fetched_at"],
                    confidence=r["confidence"], detail={"action_type": p.get("action_type")}))

        elif intent == "drugs_for_target" and target:
            for r in self._rows("""
                SELECT e.id, e.confidence, e.props, e.source_record,
                       e.source_fetched_at, n.label AS drug, n.props AS dprops, n.id AS did
                FROM edge e JOIN node n ON n.id = e.src
                WHERE e.rel='ACTS_ON' AND e.dst=? AND e.retired_at IS NULL LIMIT ?
            """, (target["id"], limit)):
                dp = json.loads(r["dprops"])
                trials = self.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=?"
                    " AND retired_at IS NULL", (r["did"],)).fetchone()[0]
                p = json.loads(r["props"])
                act = (p.get("action_type") or "").lower()
                ev.append(Evidence(
                    statement=f"{r['drug']} acts on {target['label']}"
                              f" ({act + '; ' if act else ''}"
                              f"max stage {dp.get('max_phase_overall') or 'n/a'};"
                              f" {trials} NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)",
                    edge_id=r["id"], relation="ACTS_ON", source="Open Targets / ChEMBL",
                    source_record=r["source_record"], fetched_at=r["source_fetched_at"],
                    confidence=r["confidence"],
                    detail={"drug_id": r["did"], "trials_in_graph": trials,
                            "max_phase_overall": dp.get("max_phase_overall")}))

        elif intent == "company_portfolio" and company:
            # a question about a group means the group and the companies it owns
            org_ids = self.org_family(company["id"]) + company.get("also_matching_ids", [])
            placeholders = ",".join("?" * len(org_ids))
            for r in self._rows(f"""
                SELECT e.id, e.confidence, e.props, n.label AS drug,
                       m.label AS org
                FROM edge e JOIN node n ON n.id = e.dst
                JOIN node m ON m.id = e.src
                WHERE e.rel='SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF' AND e.src IN ({placeholders})
                  AND e.retired_at IS NULL
                ORDER BY json_extract(e.props,'$.evidence_trials') DESC LIMIT ?
            """, (*org_ids, limit)):
                p = json.loads(r["props"])
                ev.append(Evidence(
                    statement=f"{r['org']} sponsors {p['evidence_trials']} "
                              f"NSCLC trial(s) evaluating {r['drug']}",
                    edge_id=r["id"], relation="SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", source="derived",
                    source_record=", ".join(p.get("evidence_sample", [])[:3]),
                    fetched_at=None, confidence=r["confidence"],
                    detail={"evidence_trials": p["evidence_trials"],
                            "example_trials": p.get("evidence_sample", [])[:3]}))

        elif intent == "drug_profile" and drug:
            d = self.dic["drugs"][drug["id"]]
            trials = self.conn.execute(
                "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=?"
                " AND retired_at IS NULL", (drug["id"],)).fetchone()[0]
            ev.append(Evidence(
                statement=f"{d['name']} is a {d['drug_type'] or 'substance'}, "
                          f"max clinical stage {d['max_phase_overall'] or 'n/a'}, "
                          f"appearing in {trials} NSCLC trial(s) in this graph",
                edge_id=f"profile:{drug['id']}", relation="PROFILE",
                source="Open Targets / ChEMBL", source_record=drug["id"],
                fetched_at=None, confidence=1.0,
                detail={"aliases_known": len(d["aliases"]),
                        "mechanisms": d["mechanisms"][:3]}))
            ev.extend(self.retrieve("targets_of_drug", entities, limit=6))
        return ev

    # -- answering --------------------------------------------------------- #
    def answer(self, question: str, use_llm: bool | None = None) -> Answer:
        entities = self.link_entities(question)
        intent = self.classify(question, entities)
        evidence = self.retrieve(intent, entities)

        if intent == "unsupported" or not evidence:
            text = ("This graph cannot answer that. It covers companies, drugs, "
                    "targets and trials in non-small cell lung cancer only, and "
                    "it answers questions about which of those relate to which. "
                    "No entity in the question matched the graph."
                    if not entities else
                    "The entities in the question were recognised but the graph "
                    "holds no edges that answer it.")
            return Answer(question, intent, entities, text, [],
                          "refusal (no supporting evidence)",
                          {"grounded": True, "unsupported_claims": [],
                           "note": "refusing is the correct output when evidence is absent"})

        if use_llm is None:
            use_llm = bool(os.environ.get("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"))
        if use_llm:
            # The model does not write the answer. It returns claims, each citing an
            # edge; the claims are checked exactly against the graph and the answer is
            # written from the accepted ones (structured.py).
            raw, by = self._llm_claims(question, evidence)
            result = self.verify_structured(raw, evidence)
            text = result["text"] or "None of the model's claims could be verified."
            if result["rejected"]:
                text += (f"\n\n({len(result['rejected'])} claim(s) were removed because "
                         "the graph does not support them.)")
            checks = {"grounded": result["verified"], "applicable": True,
                      "method": "structured claims checked edge by edge against the graph",
                      "accepted": len(result["accepted"]), "rejected": result["rejected"],
                      "unsupported_claims": [{"kind": "rejected_claim", "value":
                                              json.dumps(r["claim"])[:120], "why": r["why"]}
                                             for r in result["rejected"]]}
            return Answer(question, intent, entities, text, evidence, by, checks)

        text, by = self._template_answer(question, intent, entities, evidence)
        # the trailing scope line is generated metadata, not a claim about the
        # world, so it is excluded from the groundedness check
        claim_text = text.split("\nScope:")[0]
        checks = self.check_groundedness(claim_text, evidence, entities,
                                         generated=False,
                                         vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
                                         linker=self.link_entities)
        return Answer(question, intent, entities, text, evidence, by, checks)

    def verify_structured(self, payload, evidence) -> dict:
        """Check a structured answer against this question's evidence (structured.py)."""
        from structured import Verifier
        return Verifier(self).verify(payload, evidence)

    @staticmethod
    def _template_answer(question, intent, entities, evidence) -> tuple[str, str]:
        head = {
            # headings name the list without stating a relation of their own, so
            # the claim check can read each line under them as it stands
            "companies_for_drug": "Lead sponsorship of trials of this drug",
            "trials_for_entity": "Trials of this drug",
            "targets_of_drug": "Reported mechanism",
            "drugs_for_target": "Agents for this target",
            "company_portfolio": "This organisation's NSCLC trials",
            "drug_profile": "Profile",
        }.get(intent, "Findings")
        lines = [f"{head}:"]
        for e in evidence:
            lines.append(f"  - {e.statement} {e.citation()}")
        lines.append("")
        lines.append(f"Scope: {len(evidence)} edge(s) from this graph; "
                     "non-small cell lung cancer only.")
        return "\n".join(lines), "deterministic template (no language model)"

    @staticmethod
    def _llm_claims(question, evidence) -> tuple[str, str]:
        """Ask the model for claims, not prose. Its reply is data to be checked."""
        try:
            import anthropic
        except ImportError:
            return ('{"claims": []}', "error: the anthropic package is not installed")
        block = "\n".join(f"- id={e.edge_id} | {e.statement}" for e in evidence)
        prompt = (
            "You answer a question about a non-small cell lung cancer knowledge graph "
            "by choosing facts from the evidence below. Reply with JSON only, of the form "
            '{"claims": [...]}, where each claim is one of:\n'
            '{"kind": "relation", "relation": "ACTS_ON" | "EVALUATES" | "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", '
            '"subject": name, "object": name, "evidence": id, "action": optional, '
            '"trial_count": optional integer}\n'
            '{"kind": "trial", "trial": NCT id, "evidence": id, "phase": optional list, '
            '"status": optional}\n'
            '{"kind": "drug", "drug": name, "evidence": id, "max_stage": optional, '
            '"trials_in_graph": optional integer}\n'
            "Use the names and ids exactly as written in the evidence. Add no other keys "
            "and no prose. If the evidence does not answer the question, reply "
            '{"claims": []}.\n\n'
            f"Question: {question}\n\nEvidence:\n{block}\n")
        client = anthropic.Anthropic()
        msg = client.messages.create(
            model="claude-sonnet-4-5", max_tokens=1500,
            messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}])
        raw = msg.content[0].text.strip()
        m = re.search(r"\{.*\}", raw, re.S)            # tolerate a code fence around it
        return (m.group(0) if m else raw), "language model, structured claims"

    # -- answer quality ---------------------------------------------------- #
    @staticmethod
    def check_groundedness(text: str, evidence: list[Evidence],
                           entities: list[dict], generated: bool = True,
                           vocabulary: set | None = None, linker=None) -> dict:
        """Report what in a *generated* answer the retrieved evidence does not support.

        Two things this check is not, both found by an adversarial review and
        written down here rather than papered over:

        1. It is meaningless on the template answer. The template is built by
           concatenating the evidence statements, so checking it against those
           same statements is circular -- it cannot fail, and reporting
           `grounded=True` for it would be a test that always passes. When the
           answer was rendered rather than generated, the check reports
           `applicable=False` and asserts nothing.
        2. It is a reader of word order, not of meaning. Without a `linker` it
           only checks that capitalised entities, drug-shaped names and numbers
           appear somewhere in the retrieved set. With one (the query layer's
           entity linker) it also reads each clause into subject, relation and
           object (claims.check_claims) and reports relations, values and words
           the evidence does not give for those entities. Sentence shapes it was
           not written for can still be misread; claims.py and README known
           defect 13 list what is known to get through.
        """
        if not generated:
            return {
                "grounded": None,
                "applicable": False,
                "unsupported_claims": [],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence),
                "method": "not applicable",
                "why": ("the answer was rendered directly from the retrieved "
                        "evidence, so checking it against that evidence is "
                        "circular and would always pass"),
            }

        if linker is not None:
            # a citation whose every id is a record of this evidence is a citation,
            # not a claim; anything else in brackets stays and is checked
            from claims import _strip_citations
            known = {x.strip().lower() for e in evidence
                     for part in (e.source_record or "", e.edge_id or "", e.statement or "")
                     for x in re.split(r"[,;\s|]+", part) if x.strip()}
            text = _strip_citations(text, known)

        haystack = " ".join(
            [e.statement for e in evidence]
            + [str(v) for e in evidence for v in e.detail.values()]
            + [e.source_record or "" for e in evidence]
            + [en["label"] for en in entities]
        ).lower()
        # Words are tokenised so "47" is not satisfied by NCT02303977. Numbers
        # are tokenised separately and whole, decimals included: splitting on
        # non-alphanumerics turned evidence "47.5" into the tokens "47" and
        # "5", so an invented "47" passed and the exact "47.5" was rejected.
        haystack_tokens = set(re.findall(r"[a-z0-9]+", haystack))
        haystack_numbers = {n.replace(",", "") for n in RE_NUMBER.findall(haystack)}

        unsupported: list[dict] = []

        def alias_in_evidence(word: str) -> bool:
            if linker is None:
                return False
            return any(e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company")
                       and e["label"].lower() in haystack for e in linker(word))

        # "phase 3" is stated as PHASE3 in the evidence, so a phase number is
        # matched against that form, not against the bare figures
        haystack_flat = re.sub(r"[\s_\-]+", "", haystack)
        phase_nums = {n for m in re.finditer(r"\bphase\s*(\d)(?:\s*/\s*(\d))?\b", text, re.I)
                      for n in m.groups() if n}
        # digits inside a linked name ("AZD 9291") are part of the name, not a figure
        number_text = text
        if linker is not None:
            for _ in range(8):           # the linker reports each entity once
                before = number_text
                for e in linker(number_text):
                    mt = e.get("matched_text") or ""
                    if mt and e.get("type") != "Refused" and re.search(r"\d", mt):
                        number_text = re.sub(r"[\W_]+".join(map(re.escape, mt.split())),
                                             " ", number_text, flags=re.I)
                if number_text == before:
                    break
        for num in {n.rstrip(",") for n in RE_NUMBER.findall(number_text)}:
            if num in phase_nums and (linker is not None or f"phase{num}" in haystack_flat):
                continue      # with a linker, claims.py checks the phase per entity
            if num.replace(",", "") not in haystack_numbers:
                unsupported.append({"kind": "number", "value": num,
                                    "why": "not present in the retrieved evidence"})

        # A figure written in words cannot be matched against the evidence, so
        # it is reported as unverifiable rather than allowed through.
        for word in set(RE_NUMBER_WORD.findall(text.lower())):
            unsupported.append({"kind": "number_in_words", "value": word,
                                "why": "a figure written in words cannot be "
                                       "checked against the evidence"})

        capitalised = set(re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}\b", text))
        stop = {"Scope", "Organisations", "Trials", "Profile", "Findings",
                "Agents", "Reported", "This", "The", "None", "NSCLC", "What",
                "There", "These", "That", "Those", "According", "Sponsors",
                "They", "Them", "Their", "Its"}
        for name in capitalised - stop:
            if name.lower() not in haystack and not alias_in_evidence(name):
                unsupported.append({"kind": "entity", "value": name,
                                    "why": "named in the answer but not in the evidence"})

        # Drug names are usually lowercase in prose ("xyzabumab cures cancer"
        # passed the capitalised-only check). Anything shaped like an INN or a
        # development code must appear in the evidence too.
        for name in set(RE_DRUGLIKE.findall(text)):
            if (name.lower() not in haystack_tokens and name.lower() not in haystack
                    and not alias_in_evidence(name)):
                unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_like_name", "value": name,
                                    "why": "looks like a drug name or code but "
                                           "is not in the evidence"})

        # Any word the drug vocabulary knows ("aspirin") is a drug name,
        # whatever its shape, and must be in the evidence.
        if vocabulary:
            for w in set(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}", text)):
                if (T.canonical_key(w) in vocabulary and w.lower() not in haystack
                        and not alias_in_evidence(w)):
                    unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_name", "value": w,
                                        "why": "a known drug name not in the evidence"})

        # Figures in forms the number matcher does not read: scientific
        # notation ("1e6") and Unicode fractions or superscripts ("⅔").
        for odd in set(RE_ODD_NUMBER.findall(text)):
            unsupported.append({"kind": "number_unreadable", "value": odd,
                                "why": "a figure in a form the check cannot match"})

        if linker is not None:
            from claims import check_claims
            unsupported.extend(check_claims(text, evidence, linker, entities))

        # one finding per value, first reason kept, in a stable order
        seen_values: set[str] = set()
        key = lambda u: (str(u["value"]), u.get("sentence", ""))
        unsupported = [u for u in sorted(unsupported, key=lambda u: (*key(u), u["kind"]))
                       if not (key(u) in seen_values or seen_values.add(key(u)))]

        return {
            "grounded": not unsupported,
            "applicable": True,
            "unsupported_claims": unsupported,
            "evidence_count": len(evidence),
            "method": ("entity and tokenised-numeric overlap against the "
                       "retrieved evidence" + ("; sentence claims checked against "
                       "edges and entity-scoped wording" if linker is not None
                       else "; relations not checked (no linker)")),
        }


RE_ODD_NUMBER = re.compile(r"\d+(?:\.\d+)?[eE][+\-]?\d+|[\u00bc-\u00be\u2150-\u215e\u00b2\u00b3\u00b9\u2070-\u2079]")
RE_NUMBER = re.compile(r"(?<![\w.])\d[\d,]{0,8}(?:\.\d+)?(?![\w])")
RE_NUMBER_WORD = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:zero|one|two|three|four|five|six|seven|eight|nine|ten|eleven|"
    r"twelve|thirteen|fourteen|fifteen|sixteen|seventeen|eighteen|nineteen|"
    r"twenty|thirty|forty|fifty|sixty|seventy|eighty|ninety|hundred|thousand|"
    r"million|half|third|quarter|percent)\b")
# INN stems common in oncology, plus development codes (letters, optional
# hyphen, digits: "AZD9291", "MK-3475", "CC-486")
RE_DRUGLIKE = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:[a-z]{3,}(?:mab|nib|tinib|parib|ciclib|lisib|platin|taxel|rubicin|"
    r"mycin|fosfamide|trexate|citabine|abine|tecan|stat|zomib|degib|rafenib|"
    r"ximab|zumab|umab|limus|mustine)|[A-Za-z]{1,5}-?\d{2,6}[A-Za-z]?)\b")


def main() -> None:
    questions = sys.argv[1:] or [
        "Which companies are developing osimertinib?",
        "What trials evaluate Tagrisso?",
        "What does osimertinib target?",
        "Which drugs act on ALK?",
        "What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?",
        "What is the capital of France?",
    ]
    kg = OncoKG()
    out = []
    for q in questions:
        a = kg.answer(q)
        out.append(a.to_dict())
        print("=" * 72)
        print("Q:", q)
        print(f"   intent={a.intent}  entities="
              f"{[(e['type'], e['label']) for e in a.entities]}")
        print(f"   generated_by={a.generated_by}")
        print(a.text)
        g = a.checks
        print(f"   grounded={g['grounded']}  unsupported={g['unsupported_claims']}")
    (C.REPORTS / "query_examples.json").write_text(
        json.dumps(out, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False), encoding="utf-8")
    print("\n->", (C.REPORTS / "query_examples.json").relative_to(C.ROOT))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

2: 
3: Reading free prose for unsupported claims is a pattern match over sentence shapes,
4: and six adversarial rounds kept finding shapes it misread (see claims.py and README
5: known defect 13). This module removes the problem instead of chasing it: a language
6: model does not write the answer, it fills in claims, and the program writes the
7: answer from the claims that check out.
8: 
9: A claim is a small JSON object of a fixed kind with fixed keys:
10: 
11:   {"kind": "relation", "relation": "ACTS_ON", "subject": "osimertinib",
12:    "object": "EGFR", "evidence": "<edge id>", "action": "INHIBITOR"}          (action optional)
13:   {"kind": "relation", "relation": "EVALUATES", "subject": "NCT03909334",
14:    "object": "osimertinib", "evidence": "<edge id>"}
15:   {"kind": "relation", "relation": "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF", "subject": "AstraZeneca",
16:    "object": "osimertinib", "evidence": "<edge id>", "trial_count": 14}       (trial_count optional)
17:   {"kind": "trial", "trial": "NCT03909334", "evidence": "<edge id>",
18:    "phase": ["PHASE2"], "status": "ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING"}                    (phase, status optional; one needed)
19:   {"kind": "drug", "drug": "osimertinib", "evidence": "<edge id>",
20:    "max_stage": "APPROVAL", "trials_in_graph": 118}                           (one of the two needed)
21: 
22: A claim is accepted only when all of these hold:
23: 
24:   * its kind and keys are exactly as above (an unknown key rejects the claim, so no
25:     free text can ride along) and every value has the expected type;
26:   * the cited evidence id is one of the edges retrieved for this question, and that
27:     edge is live in the graph;
28:   * the edge's relation is the claimed relation, and its two ends are the entities
29:     the claim names (a name must resolve, as a whole, to exactly that node id);
30:   * every value equals what the graph stores: the edge's action type, the sponsor's
31:     trial count, the trial's phase set and status, the drug's maximum stage, and the
32:     number of live trials evaluating the drug.
33: 
34: The answer shown to the reader is written by `render` from accepted claims only, using
35: the graph's own labels and values, never the claim's strings. A claim that does not
36: check out is reported with the reason and left out of the answer.
37: 
38: What this does not do: it cannot tell whether the claims chosen answer the question
39: well, or whether something important was left out. It guarantees that what is said is
40: in the graph, not that everything in the graph that matters was said.
41: """

52:                  {"action", "trial_count"}),

62: 
63: 
64: def _flat(s: str) -> str:
65:     return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", " ", s.lower()).strip()
66: 
67: 
68: class Verifier:
69:     def __init__(self, kg):
70:         self.kg = kg
71: 
72:     # -- entity names --------------------------------------------------------- #
73:     def resolve(self, name, want: str) -> tuple[set[str], str | None]:
74:         """Node ids a name stands for, as a whole, among nodes of type `want`."""
75:         if not isinstance(name, str) or not name.strip() or len(name) > 120:
76:             return set(), "the name must be a short non-empty string"
77:         name = name.strip()
78:         row = self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT id, type FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
79:                                    (name,)).fetchone()
80:         if row:
81:             return ({row["id"]}, None) if row["type"] == want else (
82:                 set(), f"{name} is a {row['type'].lower()}, not a {want.lower()}")
83:         if want == "Trial":
84:             return set(), "a trial is named by its registry id (NCT followed by 8 digits)"
85:         ents = [e for e in self.kg.link_entities(name) if e.get("type") not in ("Disease",)]
86:         whole = len(ents) == 1 and _flat(ents[0].get("matched_text") or "") == _flat(name)
87:         if whole and ents[0].get("type") != want:
88:             return set(), f"{name} is a {ents[0]['type'].lower()}, not a {want.lower()}"
89:         if not whole:
90:             return set(), f"'{name}' does not name exactly one {want.lower()} on its own"
91:         e = ents[0]
92:         return {e["id"], *e.get("also_matching_ids", [])}, None
93: 
94:     # -- one claim ------------------------------------------------------------ #
95:     def check(self, claim, retrieved: set[str]) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None]:
96:         """Return (facts to render, None) or (None, reason)."""
97:         if not isinstance(claim, dict):
98:             return None, "a claim must be a JSON object"
99:         kind = claim.get("kind")
100:         if kind not in KEYS:
101:             return None, f"unknown kind {kind!r}; use relation, trial or drug"
102:         need, may = KEYS[kind]
103:         keys = set(claim)
104:         if need - keys:
105:             return None, f"missing {sorted(need - keys)}"
106:         if keys - need - may:
107:             return None, f"unknown keys {sorted(keys - need - may)} (no free text is accepted)"
108:         ev = claim["evidence"]
109:         if not isinstance(ev, str) or ev not in retrieved:
110:             return None, "the cited evidence is not among the edges retrieved for this question"
111:         edge = self.kg.conn.execute(
112:             "SELECT * FROM edge WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL", (ev,)).fetchone()
113:         if kind == "drug" and edge is None and ev.startswith("profile:"):
114:             edge = {"id": ev, "rel": "PROFILE", "src": ev.split(":", 1)[1], "dst": None,
115:                     "props": "{}"}
116:         if edge is None:
117:             return None, "the cited evidence is not a live edge of the graph"
118:         return getattr(self, "_" + kind)(claim, edge)
119: 
120:     def _relation(self, c, edge):
121:         rel = c["relation"]
122:         if rel not in RELATIONS:
123:             return None, f"unknown relation {rel!r}"
124:         if edge["rel"] != rel:
125:             return None, f"the cited edge is {edge['rel']}, not {rel}"
126:         st, ot = RELATIONS[rel]
127:         s_ids, why = self.resolve(c["subject"], st)
128:         if why:
129:             return None, why
130:         o_ids, why = self.resolve(c["object"], ot)
131:         if why:
132:             return None, why
133:         if edge["src"] not in s_ids or edge["dst"] not in o_ids:
134:             return None, "the cited edge does not join these two entities in this direction"
135:         props = json.loads(edge["props"] or "{}")
136:         facts = {"kind": "relation", "relation": rel, "edge": edge["id"],
137:                  "subject": edge["src"], "object": edge["dst"]}
138:         if "action" in c:
139:             if rel != "ACTS_ON" or not isinstance(c["action"], str):
140:                 return None, "an action belongs to an ACTS_ON claim, as a string"
141:             if _norm(c["action"]) != _norm(props.get("action_type") or ""):
142:                 return None, (f"the graph gives the action as {props.get('action_type') or 'none'}, "
143:                               f"not {c['action']}")
144:             facts["action"] = props["action_type"]
145:         if "trial_count" in c:
146:             n = c["trial_count"]
147:             if rel != "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF" or type(n) is not int:
148:                 return None, "a trial_count belongs to a SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF claim, as a whole number"
149:             if n != props.get("evidence_trials"):
150:                 return None, f"the graph gives {props.get('evidence_trials')} trials, not {n}"
151:             facts["trial_count"] = n
152:         return facts, None
153: 
154:     def _trial(self, c, edge):
155:         if edge["rel"] != "EVALUATES":
156:             return None, "a trial's phase or status is cited from an EVALUATES edge of that trial"
157:         ids, why = self.resolve(c["trial"], "Trial")
158:         if why:
159:             return None, why
160:         if edge["src"] not in ids:
161:             return None, "the cited edge belongs to a different trial"
162:         if "phase" not in c and "status" not in c:
163:             return None, "a trial claim states a phase, a status or both"
164:         node = self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT props FROM node WHERE id=?", (edge["src"],)).fetchone()
165:         props = json.loads(node["props"] or "{}")
166:         facts = {"kind": "trial", "edge": edge["id"], "trial": edge["src"]}
167:         if "phase" in c:
168:             ph = c["phase"]
169:             if not isinstance(ph, list) or not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in ph):
170:                 return None, "phase is a list of strings, e.g. [\"PHASE2\"]"
171:             if sorted(_norm(x) for x in ph) != sorted(_norm(x) for x in props.get("phases") or []):
172:                 return None, f"the graph gives the phase as {props.get('phases')}, not {ph}"
173:             facts["phase"] = props.get("phases")
174:         if "status" in c:
175:             if not isinstance(c["status"], str) or _norm(c["status"]) != _norm(props.get("status") or ""):
176:                 return None, f"the graph gives the status as {props.get('status')}, not {c['status']}"
177:             facts["status"] = props.get("status")
178:         return facts, None
179: 
180:     def _drug(self, c, edge):
181:         ids, why = self.resolve(c["drug"], "Drug")
182:         if why:
183:             return None, why
184:         if edge["rel"] not in ("ACTS_ON", "PROFILE") or edge["src"] not in ids:
185:             return None, "a drug's stage or trial count is cited from that drug's own statement"
186:         if "max_stage" not in c and "trials_in_graph" not in c:
187:             return None, "a drug claim states max_stage, trials_in_graph or both"
188:         node = self.kg.conn.execute("SELECT props FROM node WHERE id=?", (edge["src"],)).fetchone()
189:         props = json.loads(node["props"] or "{}")
190:         facts = {"kind": "drug", "edge": edge["id"], "drug": edge["src"]}
191:         if "max_stage" in c:
192:             if not isinstance(c["max_stage"], str) or \
193:                     _norm(c["max_stage"]) != _norm(props.get("max_phase_overall") or ""):
194:                 return None, (f"the graph gives the maximum stage as "
195:                               f"{props.get('max_phase_overall')}, not {c['max_stage']}")
196:             facts["max_stage"] = props.get("max_phase_overall")
197:         if "trials_in_graph" in c:
198:             n = c["trials_in_graph"]
199:             real = self.kg.conn.execute(
200:                 "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
201:                 (edge["src"],)).fetchone()[0]
202:             if type(n) is not int or n != real:
203:                 return None, f"the graph gives {real} trials evaluating this drug, not {n}"
204:             facts["trials_in_graph"] = real
205:         return facts, None
206: 
207:     # -- a whole answer -------------------------------------------------------- #
208:     def verify(self, payload, evidence) -> dict:
209:         """`payload` is the model's JSON (a string or already parsed)."""
210:         if isinstance(payload, str):
211:             try:
212:                 payload = json.loads(payload)
213:             except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
214:                 return {"verified": False, "accepted": [], "text": "",
215:                         "rejected": [{"claim": None, "why": f"not valid JSON: {e.msg}"}]}
216:         claims = payload.get("claims") if isinstance(payload, dict) else None
217:         if not isinstance(claims, list) or not claims or set(payload) != {"claims"}:
218:             return {"verified": False, "accepted": [], "text": "",
219:                     "rejected": [{"claim": None,
220:                                   "why": 'the answer must be {"claims": [...]} and nothing else'}]}
221:         retrieved = {e.edge_id for e in evidence}
222:         accepted, rejected = [], []
223:         for c in claims[:50]:
224:             facts, why = self.check(c, retrieved)
225:             if why:
226:                 rejected.append({"claim": c, "why": why})
227:             else:
228:                 accepted.append(facts)
229:         if len(claims) > 50:
230:             rejected.append({"claim": None, "why": "more than 50 claims; the rest were not read"})
231:         return {"verified": not rejected, "accepted": accepted, "rejected": rejected,
232:                 "text": render(accepted, self.kg.conn)}
233: 
234: 
235: def render(facts: list[dict], conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> str:
236:     """Sentences from accepted facts, in the graph's own words."""
237:     def label(i):
238:         r = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=?", (i,)).fetchone()
239:         return r[0] if r else i
240: 
241:     lines = []
242:     for f in facts:
243:         if f["kind"] == "relation" and f["relation"] == "ACTS_ON":
244:             act = f" ({f['action'].lower()})" if f.get("action") else ""
245:             lines.append(f"{label(f['subject'])} acts on {label(f['object'])}{act}")
246:         elif f["kind"] == "relation" and f["relation"] == "EVALUATES":
247:             lines.append(f"{label(f['subject'])} evaluates {label(f['object'])}")
248:         elif f["kind"] == "relation":
249:             n = f" {f['trial_count']}" if "trial_count" in f else ""
250:             lines.append(f"{label(f['subject'])} is lead sponsor of{n} trial(s) evaluating "
251:                          f"{label(f['object'])} - a sponsorship, not evidence of developing "
252:                          "the drug")
253:         elif f["kind"] == "trial":
254:             bits = []
255:             if "phase" in f:
256:                 bits.append("phase " + ", ".join(f["phase"]) if f["phase"] else "no phase given")
257:             if "status" in f:
258:                 bits.append("status " + f["status"])
259:             lines.append(f"{label(f['trial'])}: " + "; ".join(bits))
260:         else:
261:             bits = []
262:             if "max_stage" in f:
263:                 bits.append(f"maximum clinical stage {f['max_stage']}")
264:             if "trials_in_graph" in f:
265:                 bits.append(f"{f['trials_in_graph']} NSCLC trial(s) in this graph")
266:             lines.append(f"{label(f['drug'])}: " + "; ".join(bits))
267:         lines[-1] += f" [{f['edge']}]"
268:     return "\n".join(f"- {x}" for x in lines)
