FILE src/query.py
1: """Grounded question answering over the graph.
2: 
3: The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
4: question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
5: graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
6: dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.
7: 
8: Three properties the answering layer holds to:
9: 
10: * **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
11:   each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
12: * **The graph answers, not the model.** The language model, when a key is
13:   present, is given the retrieved evidence and nothing else; with no key the
14:   same evidence is rendered directly. The facts are identical either way, so the
15:   model can only change the prose.
16: * **The answer is checked against its own evidence.** Entities and numbers in
17:   the generated text are verified against the retrieved set, and anything
18:   unsupported is reported rather than shipped. An unverifiable answer is worse
19:   than a short one.
20: """
21: from __future__ import annotations
22: 
23: import json
24: import os
25: import re
26: import sqlite3
27: import sys
28: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
29: from pathlib import Path
30: 
31: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
32: import config as C
33: import normalize as N
34: import textrules as T
35: 
36: DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
37: TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"
38: 
39: 
40: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
41: # evidence
42: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
43: @dataclass
44: class Evidence:
45:     statement: str
46:     edge_id: str
47:     relation: str
48:     source: str
49:     source_record: str | None
50:     fetched_at: str | None
51:     confidence: float
52:     tier: str | None = None
53:     url: str | None = None
54:     detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
55: 
56:     def citation(self) -> str:
57:         base = self.source_record or self.source
58:         return f"[{base}]"
59: 
60: 
61: @dataclass
62: class Answer:
63:     question: str
64:     intent: str
65:     entities: list[dict]
66:     text: str
67:     evidence: list[Evidence]
68:     generated_by: str
69:     checks: dict
70: 
71:     def to_dict(self) -> dict:
72:         return {
73:             "question": self.question,
74:             "intent": self.intent,
75:             "entities": self.entities,
76:             "answer": self.text,
77:             "generated_by": self.generated_by,
78:             "checks": self.checks,
79:             "evidence": [
80:                 {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
81:                  "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
82:                  "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
83:                  "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
84:                 for e in self.evidence
85:             ],
86:         }
87: 
88: 
89: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
90: # engine
91: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
92: class OncoKG:
93:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
94:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
95:         self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
96:         self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
97:         resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
98:         self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
99:         self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
100:         self.disease = resolved["disease"]
101:         self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
102:                         if t.get("symbol")}
103:         # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
104:         # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
105:         self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
106:         for row in self.conn.execute(
107:                 "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
108:             self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
109:             self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
110:         # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
111:         for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
112:             if same in self.companies:
113:                 self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
114:         self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}
115: 
116:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
117:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
118:         found: list[dict] = []
119:         seen: set = set()
120:         tokens = [t.strip(" ?.,!;:") for t in re.split(r"[\s,]+", question)
121:                   if t.strip(" ?.,!;:")]
122: 
123:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too. Linking straight
124:         # against the index let "Mvasi" or "liposomal doxorubicin" answer as
125:         # the originator molecule. A token naming a distinct product is
126:         # refused; a qualifier ("liposomal", "biosimilar") takes its
127:         # neighbours with it.
128:         refused: set[int] = set()
129:         for i, tok in enumerate(tokens):
130:             if T.is_distinct_product(tok) or T.is_radiotracer(tok):
131:                 refused.add(i)
132:                 if not T.canonical_key(tok) in self.dic["index"]:
133:                     refused.update({i - 1, i + 1})
134:         refused = {x for x in refused if 0 <= x < len(tokens)}
135:         run: list[int] = []
136:         for i in sorted(refused) + [None]:
137:             if run and (i is None or i != run[-1] + 1):
138:                 span = " ".join(tokens[run[0]:run[-1] + 1])
139:                 found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": span,
140:                               "matched_text": span,
141:                               "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
142:                                      "the originator molecule"})
143:                 run = []
144:             if i is not None:
145:                 run.append(i)
146: 
147:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
148:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
149:                 if refused & set(range(i, i + size)):
150:                     continue
151:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
152:                 if len(window) < 3:
153:                     continue
154:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
155: 
156:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
157:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
158:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
159:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
160:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
161:                     hits = None
162:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
163:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
164:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
165:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
166:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
167:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
168:                                       "matched_text": window,
169:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
170:                     continue
171: 
172:                 if window.lower() in self.targets:
173:                     t = self.targets[window.lower()]
174:                     if ("Target", t["target_id"]) not in seen:
175:                         seen.add(("Target", t["target_id"]))
176:                         found.append({"type": "Target", "id": t["target_id"],
177:                                       "label": t["symbol"], "matched_text": window})
178:                     continue
179: 
180:                 if key in self.companies:
181:                     cid = self.companies[key][0]
182:                     if ("Company", cid) not in seen:
183:                         seen.add(("Company", cid))
184:                         row = self.conn.execute(
185:                             "SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=?", (cid,)).fetchone()
186:                         found.append({"type": "Company", "id": cid,
187:                                       "label": row["label"], "matched_text": window,
188:                                       "also_matching_ids": self.companies[key][1:]})
189:                     continue
190: 
191:                 if key in self.disease["index"]:
192:                     if ("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]) not in seen:
193:                         seen.add(("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]))
194:                         found.append({"type": "Disease",
195:                                       "id": self.disease["disease_id"],
196:                                       "label": self.disease["name"],
197:                                       "matched_text": window})
198:         return found
199: 
200:     def org_family(self, company_id: str) -> list[str]:
201:         """The organisation plus any companies a curated parent edge links to it."""
202:         ids = {company_id}
203:         for row in self.conn.execute(
204:                 "SELECT dst FROM edge WHERE rel='PARENT_OF' AND src=?"
205:                 " AND retired_at IS NULL", (company_id,)):
206:             ids.add(row["dst"])
207:         return sorted(ids)
208: 
209:     # -- intent ------------------------------------------------------------ #
210:     @staticmethod
211:     def classify(question: str, entities: list[dict]) -> str:
212:         q = question.lower()
213:         types = {e["type"] for e in entities}
214:         if re.search(r"\b(who|which compan|which firm|sponsor|develop|partner)", q):
215:             return "companies_for_drug" if "Drug" in types else "companies_for_target"
216:         if re.search(r"\b(trials?|stud(y|ies)|nct)\b", q):
217:             return "trials_for_entity"
218:         if re.search(r"\b(target|mechanism|acts? on|moa)\b", q) and "Drug" in types:
219:             return "targets_of_drug"
220:         if "Target" in types and re.search(r"\b(drug|inhibitor|agent|candidate)", q):
221:             return "drugs_for_target"
222:         if "Company" in types:
223:             return "company_portfolio"
224:         if "Drug" in types:
225:             return "drug_profile"
226:         if "Target" in types:
227:             return "drugs_for_target"
228:         return "unsupported"
229: 
230:     # -- retrieval --------------------------------------------------------- #
231:     def _rows(self, sql: str, params: tuple) -> list[sqlite3.Row]:
232:         return list(self.conn.execute(sql, params))
233: 
234:     def retrieve(self, intent: str, entities: list[dict], limit: int = 12):
235:         ev: list[Evidence] = []
236:         drug = next((e for e in entities if e["type"] == "Drug"), None)
237:         target = next((e for e in entities if e["type"] == "Target"), None)
238:         company = next((e for e in entities if e["type"] == "Company"), None)
239: 
240:         if intent == "companies_for_drug" and drug:
241:             for r in self._rows("""
242:                 SELECT e.id, e.src, e.confidence, e.props, n.label AS company
243:                 FROM edge e JOIN node n ON n.id = e.src
244:                 WHERE e.rel='SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF' AND e.dst=? AND e.retired_at IS NULL
245:                 ORDER BY json_extract(e.props,'$.evidence_trials') DESC LIMIT ?

FILE src/app.py
1: """A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.
2: 
3:     python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765
4: 
5: Four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
6: how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
7: evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
8: claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
9: see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All three call
10: the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
11: OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
12: pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
13: """
14: from __future__ import annotations
15: 
16: import json
17: import sqlite3
18: import sys
19: import threading
20: from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
21: from pathlib import Path
22: 
23: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
24: import config as C
25: import normalize as N
26: from query import OncoKG
27: 
28: PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
29: PORT = 8765
30: 
31: 
32: class Engine:
33:     def __init__(self):
34:         self.kg = OncoKG()
35:         # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
36:         # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
37:         self.kg.conn.close()
38:         self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
39:         self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
40:         self.lock = threading.Lock()
41:         self.dic = self.kg.dic
42:         self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
43:         target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
44:         self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
45: 
46:     def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
47:         out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
48:         for c in out["components"]:
49:             d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
50:             if d:
51:                 c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
52:                              ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
53:                 c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
54:                                            for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
55:                 c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
56:                     "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
57:                     (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
58:                 par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
59:                 if par:
60:                     c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
61:                         "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
62:                         (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
63:             if c.get("candidates"):
64:                 c["candidate_names"] = [
65:                     (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
66:         return out
67: 
68:     def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
69:         return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
70: 
71:     def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
72:         """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
73:         kg = self.kg
74:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
75:         evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
76:         res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
77:         res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
78:         return res
79: 
80:     def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
81:         """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
82:         kg = self.kg
83:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
84:         intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
85:         evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
86:         checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
87:                                        vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
88:                                        linker=kg.link_entities)
89:         return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
90:                 "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
91:                               "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
92:                 "evidence_count": len(evidence)}
93: 
94: 
95: def make_handler(engine: Engine):
96:     class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
97:         def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
98:             self.send_response(code)
99:             self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
100:             self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
101:             self.end_headers()
102:             self.wfile.write(body)
103: 
104:         def do_GET(self):
105:             if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
106:                 self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
107:             else:
108:                 self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")
109: 
110:         def do_POST(self):
111:             try:
112:                 n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
113:                 body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
114:                 text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()[:500]
115:                 if not text:
116:                     raise ValueError("empty input")
117:                 if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
118:                                      "/api/structured"):
119:                     return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
120:                 with engine.lock:
121:                     if self.path == "/api/resolve":
122:                         res = engine.resolve(text)
123:                     elif self.path == "/api/ask":
124:                         res = engine.ask(text)
125:                     elif self.path == "/api/structured":
126:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
127:                         if not answer:
128:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
129:                         res = engine.structured(text, answer)
130:                     else:
131:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
132:                         if not answer:
133:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
134:                         res = engine.check(text, answer)
135:                 self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
136:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
137:             except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
138:                 self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
139:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
140: 
141:         def log_message(self, *a):
142:             pass
143:     return Handler
144: 
145: 
146: def main() -> None:
147:     port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
148:     engine = Engine()
149:     print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
150:     ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()
151: 
152: 
153: if __name__ == "__main__":
154:     main()

339: def canonical_key(text: str) -> str:
340:     """Aggressive key used for dictionary lookup (never shown to a user)."""
341:     t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
342:     t = "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c))
343:     t = t.lower()
344:     t = t.replace("’", "'").replace("‐", "-").replace("–", "-").replace("—", "-")
345:     t = re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "", t)
346:     return t

+"""Independent structured-answer adversarial checks; never writes the source graph."""
+import copy
+import hashlib
+import json
+import sqlite3
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+import query
+from structured import Verifier
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+kg = query.OncoKG()
+kg.conn.execute('PRAGMA query_only=ON')
+q = 'What does osimertinib target?'
+entities = kg.link_entities(q)
+ev = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(q, entities), entities)
+base = {'kind': 'relation', 'relation': 'ACTS_ON', 'subject': 'osimertinib',
+        'object': 'EGFR', 'evidence': ev[0].edge_id, 'action': 'INHIBITOR'}
+results = []
+
+def probe(tag, payload, evidence=ev):
+    row = {'case': tag, 'question': q, 'input': payload}
+    try:
+        row['output'] = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
+    except Exception as exc:
+        row['exception'] = {'type': type(exc).__name__, 'message': str(exc)}
+    results.append(row)
+
+probe('baseline', {'claims': [base]})
+for name in [' osimertinib ', 'OSIMERTINIB', 'osimertinib is safe',
+             'osimertinib\u662f\u5b89\u5168\u7684', '\u975eosimertinib',
+             'osimertinib\uac00 \uc544\ub2cc', 'osimertinib\u200b',
+             'osimertinib / placebo', 'osimertinib,osimertinib',
+             'osimertinib\x00', 'osimertinib' + ' ' * 120,
+             'o' * 121, 'ＯＳＩＭＥＲＴＩＮＩＢ']:
+    c = dict(base, subject=name)
+    probe('name:' + repr(name), {'claims': [c]})
+values = [None, True, False, 0, 1, 1.0, '1', '', [], {}, ['ACTS_ON'],
+          ['INHIBITOR', 1], float('nan'), float('inf')]
+for key in base:
+    for value in values:
+        probe('type:' + key + ':' + repr(value), {'claims': [dict(base, **{key: value})]})
+for key in ['note', '<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>', 'text']:
+    probe('unknown_key:' + key, {'claims': [dict(base, **{key: 'untrusted'})]})
+for count in [0, 1, 50, 51, 1000]:
+    probe('claim_count:' + str(count), {'claims': [base] * count})
+for raw in ['{"claims": [', 'null', 'NaN', 'Infinity', '{}', '[]',
+            '{"claims": [' + json.dumps(base) + '], "claims": []}',
+            '{"claims": [], "claims": [' + json.dumps(base) + ']}',
+            '{"claims": [' + json.dumps(base)[:-1] + ', "action":"ACTIVATOR"}]}',
+            '{"claims": [' + json.dumps(base)[:-1] + ', "action":"INHIBITOR"}]}',
+            '[' * 1100 + '0' + ']' * 1100]:
+    probe('raw_json:' + raw[:80], raw)
+
+manifest = {'commit': 'aa5655ae89c47631fefb9c868989ba2c1befa82e',
+            'sha256': {str(p.relative_to(ROOT)): hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
+                       for p in [ROOT/'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite', ROOT/'data/processed/resolved.json',
+                                 ROOT/'src/structured.py', ROOT/'src/query.py', ROOT/'src/app.py',
+                                 ROOT/'web/try.html']}}
+(OUT/'root_manifest.json').write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+(OUT/'root_probe_results.json').write_text(json.dumps(results, ensure_ascii=True, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
+summary = {'cases': len(results), 'verified': sum(r.get('output', {}).get('verified', False) for r in results),
+           'exceptions': [{k: r[k] for k in ['case', 'exception']} for r in results if 'exception' in r],
+           'accepted_names': [{'case': r['case'], 'output': r['output']} for r in results
+                              if r['case'].startswith('name:') and r.get('output', {}).get('verified')]}
+print(json.dumps(summary, ensure_ascii=True, indent=2))

FILE README.md
443: {"claims": [
444:   {"kind": "relation", "relation": "ACTS_ON", "subject": "osimertinib", "object": "EGFR",
445:    "evidence": "CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410", "action": "INHIBITOR"},
446:   {"kind": "trial", "trial": "NCT03909334", "evidence": "NCT03909334|EVALUATES|CHEMBL3353410|NCT03909334",
447:    "phase": ["PHASE2"], "status": "ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING"}
448: ]}
449: ```
450: 
451: Each claim is accepted only if its keys are exactly those of its kind (no free-text
452: field exists, and an unknown key rejects the claim), the cited edge is one of those
453: retrieved for the question and is live, the edge's relation and both ends are the
454: ones the claim names (a name must resolve, whole, to that node), and every value
455: equals what the graph stores: action type, sponsor's trial count, a trial's phase
456: set and status, a drug's maximum stage and its number of trials. The answer is then
457: written from accepted claims in the graph's own labels and values, with the edge id
458: after each line, so nothing the model typed reaches the reader. Rejected claims are
459: listed with the reason and left out.
460: 
461: This replaced checking the model's prose. Six adversarial rounds against a prose
462: checker (`src/claims.py`, kept as an auxiliary screen) each found new sentence
463: shapes that slipped through, because a reader of word order cannot keep up with
464: English. A closed claim format can be checked exactly. What it cannot judge is
465: whether the claims chosen answer the question well or leave something important
466: out: it guarantees what is said is in the graph, not that everything that matters
467: was said.
468: 
469: With no key, the evidence is rendered directly by a template, and the prose check
470: reports `applicable: false`, because a template built from the evidence cannot
525:     existence of every subsidiary is unverified, and nothing re-checks the map
526:     when ownership changes.
527: 13. **The prose check is an auxiliary screen, not a guarantee.** Answers from the
528:     model are no longer prose but structured claims checked exactly (see "Asking it
529:     things"). `src/claims.py`, which reads free prose clause by clause against the
530:     evidence, is kept for prose from elsewhere (the try-it page's box 4). Six
531:     adversarial rounds found 33, about 40, about 25, about 20, about 15 and then 8
532:     sentence shapes that got through it (reversed nouns, clefts, pronouns, counts
533:     and phases borrowed across entities, headings, relative clauses, kind words),
534:     and its repairs began to reject faithful answers (22 of 38 in round 6). Each
535:     shape found is a regression test, but it reads word order, not meaning, and is
536:     documented as able to miss unsupported claims and to reject correct ones.
537: 14. **Provenance does not record the code version.** Snapshots carry their
538:     fetch time, but the graph does not store which revision of the rules built
539:     it, so a change that arrives with a new snapshot and a code change together
540:     cannot be separated from the database alone; only git history can.
541: 8. **The query layer answers the nearest question it can.** "Is pembrolizumab
542:    safe in patients with autoimmune disease?" returns a drug profile rather than
543:    refusing. It refuses only when no entity links at all.

309: 바뀌면 바뀐 만큼만 기록됩니다 — 파생 엣지는 기본 엣지를 정리한 뒤에 다시 계산합니다.
310: 
311: ---
312: 
313: ## 질문하기 — 언어 모델은 답을 쓰지 않고 주장을 냅니다
314: 
315: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`가 있으면 언어 모델은 답 문장을 쓰지 않고, 근거 엣지 id 를 붙인 주장 목록(JSON)을 냅니다.
316: 프로그램이 주장을 하나씩 그래프와 정확히 대조합니다(`src/structured.py`). 주장의 칸은 종류마다 정해져 있어 자유
317: 글자를 넣을 칸이 없고, 모르는 칸이 있으면 그 주장을 버립니다. 인용한 엣지는 이번 질문으로 검색된 것이어야 하고,
318: 관계와 양 끝 대상이 주장과 같아야 하며(이름은 통째로 그 노드를 가리켜야 함), 작용 종류·후원 시험 수·시험의
319: 단계와 상태·약의 최대 단계와 시험 수는 저장된 값과 같아야 합니다. 답 문장은 받아들인 주장만으로, 그래프의
320: 이름과 값으로 프로그램이 쓰고 줄마다 엣지 id 를 붙입니다. 모델이 쓴 글자는 답에 들어가지 않습니다. 이 방식이
321: 보증하는 것은 말한 내용이 그래프에 있다는 것이고, 질문에 잘 맞는 주장을 골랐는지·중요한 것을 빠뜨리지
322: 않았는지는 판단하지 못합니다.
323: 
324: ## 남아 있는 결함
325: 
326: 조용히 덮는 대신 목록으로 남깁니다. 2차 때 여기서 `adenovirus` 백신 항목을 "더 재현되지 않는다"며
327: 뺐는데, 3차 감사가 샘플의 더 긴 문자열에서 여전히 재현된다는 것을 보였습니다. 지금은 고쳤고 그

257: <section>
258:   <p class="eyebrow">06 &middot; 질문에 답하기</p>
259:   <h2>근거로만 답하고, 스스로 검사합니다</h2>
260:   <p class="lede">질문 속 약 이름도 같은 사전으로 맞추기 때문에, 상품명(예: Tagrisso)으로 물어도 표준 번호로 저장된 지도에서 찾습니다.
261:   답을 만든 뒤에는 <b>찾은 근거에 없는 약 이름이나 숫자가 답에 들어갔는지</b> 검사해서, 있으면 그대로 내보내지 않고 표시합니다.
262:   관련 없는 질문(예: 프랑스 수도)에는 답하지 않습니다.</p>
263:   <div class="plain"><b>AI가 답을 쓰면</b> &mdash; 언어 모델은 문장을 쓰지 않고 &lsquo;주장 목록&rsquo;을 냅니다: 무엇이 무엇에 작용하는지, 어떤 시험이 어떤 약을 시험하는지, 단계&middot;상태&middot;시험 수는 얼마인지, 근거 엣지 id 는 무엇인지.
264:   프로그램이 주장마다 그 엣지를 그래프에서 꺼내 관계&middot;방향&middot;값이 정확히 같은지 대조하고, 맞는 주장만으로 답 문장을 그래프의 이름과 값으로 씁니다. 모델이 쓴 글자는 답에 들어가지 않으므로 &ldquo;안전하다&rdquo; 같은 말이 끼어들 자리가 없습니다.
265:   (처음에는 AI가 쓴 문장을 단어 순서로 읽어 검사했지만, 적대 검증 여섯 차례 동안 매번 새로 빠져나가는 문장이 나와 이 방식으로 바꿨습니다. 그 검사는 보조로 남겼습니다.)
266:   이 방식이 보증하는 것은 &lsquo;말한 것은 그래프에 있다&rsquo;까지이고, 질문에 맞는 주장을 골랐는지는 사람이 봐야 합니다.
267:   지금 보이는 답은 언어 모델 없이 근거를 정해진 틀로 보여 준 것이라 검사 대상이 아닙니다.</div>

modelui_findings.md
# Model path and page review (aa5655a)

Reproduce from repo root:

```powershell
$env:PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1'
python -B audit_evidence_claims/s1/modelui_probe.py
node audit_evidence_claims/s1/modelui_page_probe.js
```

The Python harness invokes the actual `_llm_claims` and `answer(use_llm=True)` with an injected `anthropic` module, then sends real HTTP requests to `make_handler(Engine())` on an ephemeral localhost server. It puts both graph connections in SQLite `query_only` mode. It does not call the paid/network model service. The JS harness executes the actual script from `web/try.html` in a Node VM, supplies the captured HTTP JSON and minimal form/DOM objects, and observes assigned HTML. This is a JS-handler/escaping test, not full browser rendering.

## BLOCKERS

None found in the assigned model/text leakage surface. No tested unsupported model text reached the answer, and rejected HTML strings were escaped in the page.

## SHOULD FIX

1. **Malformed field types escape as exceptions rather than a rejected-claim result.** `src/structured.py:99-101` performs hash lookup before validating kind type; `src/structured.py:121-123` does the same for relation. Exact question: `What does osimertinib target?`. Exact model reply `{"claims":[{"kind":[]}]}` produces `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'` from `answer(use_llm=True)`. `{"claims":[{"kind":{}}]}` produces `TypeError: unhashable type: 'dict'`. A valid relation claim with `relation: []` also raises the list error. HTTP `/api/structured` catches these and returns 400 `{"error":"unhashable type: 'list'"}` (`src/app.py:137-139`), but direct/model callers receive no result. `faithful_then_kind_list` shows even a previously valid claim is lost. This fails closed, not false acceptance.

2. **Non-finite JSON constants break the API response contract.** Exact inner answer is `{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"osimertinib","object":"EGFR","evidence":"CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410","action":NaN}]}` and question is the one above. `json.loads` accepts `NaN` at `src/structured.py:212`; action validation correctly rejects it. However `src/app.py:135` serializes rejected claim with literal `NaN`. HTTP status is 200 but the body is not standard JSON. The page's real `r.json()` path (`web/try.html:173`) fails before showing a rejection. Observed handler HTML: `<p class="err">오류: Unexpected token 'N', ...&quot;&quot;action&quot;: NaN}, &quot;why&quot;... is not valid JSON</p>`. Infinity likewise rejects as an action in the model path. Parse non-finite tokens as invalid input before reflecting them. No unsupported claim is accepted.

3. **Empty SDK content, API failures, and extreme JSON nesting are not converted to structured failure.** At `src/query.py:460-464`, mocked SDK response `content=[]` produces `IndexError: list index out of range`; `content=[SimpleNamespace(type='tool_use')]` produces `AttributeError: 'types.SimpleNamespace' object has no attribute 'text'`; `content=[SimpleNamespace(text=None)]` produces `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'strip'`; mocked `messages.create` raising `RuntimeError('simulated SDK failure')` escapes unchanged. `src/structured.py:212-215` catches only JSONDecodeError: exact raw string expression `'{"claims":' + '['*20000 + '0' + ']'*20000 + '}'`, produces `RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded while decoding a JSON array from a unicode string`. Full bytes are in `modelui_results.json` case `nested20000`. Ordinary malformed JSON and empty text do return a closed rejection. These are robustness findings only.

## NOTES

- Baseline exact claim: `{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"osimertinib","object":"EGFR","evidence":"CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410","action":"INHIBITOR"}`. Wrapped as `{"claims":[claim]}` for the question above, actual model path returns `grounded=true, accepted=1, rejected=[]` and exactly `- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor) [CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410]`.
- The same JSON in a Markdown fence, or surrounded by arbitrary text, produces exactly the same accepted answer. The greedy brace extraction at `src/query.py:465` discards text outside the first/last braces; extra prose does not leak. Two JSON objects concatenated, or `{garbage}` followed by a valid object, reject because the extraction is not parseable JSON. This may lose otherwise useful claims but does not expand accepted claims.
- HTML probe string is exactly `<img src=x onerror="globalThis.MODEL_INJECTED=1"><script>globalThis.MODEL_INJECTED=2</script>&"'`. Supplying it as extra `note` or as `action` rejects. `web/try.html:93,176-181` escapes answer, rejection reason, rejected claim JSON, evidence, and errors. Actual handler output contained `&lt;img`/`&lt;script` and no raw `img`/`script` element. Rejected model text is intentionally visible as escaped diagnostic text, separate from the answer. `src/query.py:392-399` similarly retains rejected strings in checks, not in `Answer.text`.
- 51 copies of the baseline claim accept only 50, return `grounded=false`, and append `{"claim":null,"why":"more than 50 claims; the rest were not read"}` (`src/structured.py:223-230`). Replacing the 51st with an injected note has the same result; its content cannot reach the answer.
- Duplicate action keys are last-wins at JSON decoding: `"action":"ACTIVATOR","action":"INHIBITOR"` accepts and renders graph inhibitor; reversing the pair rejects. The discarded duplicate is not rendered. Evidence-consistent, but not strict duplicate-key rejection.
- `{"claims":[]}` is requested by the prompt (`src/query.py:457-458`) but the verifier treats it as an invalid envelope (`src/structured.py:217-220`), returns one rejection, and answer adds `(1 claim(s) were removed because the graph does not support them.)`. Nothing was actually claimed. Missing `anthropic` package follows this same route with `generated_by="error: the anthropic package is not installed"`.
- A 1,100-deep nested array rejects as a non-object claim without acceptance; a 139 KB model reply with an extra unknown field rejects. The HTTP endpoint reads at most 20,000 bytes (`src/app.py:113`), so that oversized request returns 400 truncated-JSON error rather than accepting a partial answer.
- `src/query.py:12-18` still says the model only changes prose and facts are identical; that header is stale after structured selection. `web/try.html:240` says box 3 checks an AI answer, while it now accepts claim JSON (less severe wording drift).

Artifacts: `modelui_probe.py`, `modelui_results.json` (all exact raw replies, outputs and SDK request), `modelui_prompt.txt`, `modelui_api_fixtures.json` (exact requests and raw responses), `modelui_page_probe.js`, `modelui_page_results.json` (full generated HTML), `modelui_stdout.txt`, `modelui_page_stdout.txt`. Parsed non-finite values in the portable results file are explicitly labeled strings; original raw reply/request/response strings retain exact `NaN`/`Infinity` tokens.


faithful_findings.md
﻿# Independent C/E/F review, aa5655a

No tracked files were edited. Live graph connections were made query-only. `faithful_probe.py` reproduces the main live-graph usefulness probes and `faithful_results.json` preserves exact questions, payloads, evidence and outputs. Additional outputs are in `faithful_additional.json`, `faithful_graph_dictionary.json`, and `faithful_empty_phase_fixture.json`.

## BLOCKERS

None independently identified in this C/E/F slice. Parent's independently reproduced Unicode whole-name bypass should be classified against the explicit whole-name guarantee, not as an HTML/rendering leak.

## SHOULD FIX

### Canonical evidence labels can be rejected (IDs work)

Code: `src/query.py:147` only tries entity names up to five tokens, `src/query.py:162` requires one dictionary drug ID, `src/structured.py:85-92` depends on that linker. The model prompt says use names exactly as written in evidence (`src/query.py:467-468`). Exact questions/payloads/output:

1. Question: `Which companies are developing osimertinib?`
   Input: `{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF","subject":"Second Affiliated Hospital Of Nanchang University","object":"OSIMERTINIB","evidence":"ORG:secondaffiliatedhospitalofnanchanguniversity|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL3353410|","trial_count":2}]}`.
   Output for this claim: rejected, reason `'Second Affiliated Hospital Of Nanchang University' does not name exactly one company on its own`. The seven-word `Tianjin Medical University Cancer Institute And Hospital` label similarly rejects. Both are copied exactly from retrieved evidence. Changing subject to the corresponding `ORG:...` ID accepts.
2. Question: `Which drugs act on ALK?`
   Input: `{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE","object":"ALK","evidence":"CHEMBL3707320|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000171094|CHEMBL3707320","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}`.
   Output: rejected, reason `'ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE' does not name exactly one drug on its own`. A `kind=drug` claim with this same displayed name, `max_stage=APPROVAL,trials_in_graph=1`, also rejects. Changing drug/subject to `CHEMBL3707320` accepts.
3. Outside the demos: question `What does aprinocarsen target?` yields `APRINOCARSEN acts on PRKCA (antisense inhibitor)`. Input relation `subject=APRINOCARSEN,object=PRKCA,action=ANTISENSE INHIBITOR,evidence=CHEMBL2108287|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000154229|CHEMBL2108287` rejects (`'PRKCA' does not name exactly one target on its own`). Changing object to `ENSG00000154229` accepts. `query.py:101-102` only indexes the selected target list although ACTS_ON can add additional target nodes.

These are false rejections/usefulness defects. All these facts remain expressible with IDs, so they are not unsupported assertions or an absolute schema expressiveness failure.

## NOTES

### Demo facts are expressible using IDs; rendering preserves the checked facts

Each payload includes all supported schema facts visible in the retrieved evidence, up to default 12 evidence rows. Exact inputs and full text live in `faithful_results.json`.

| Question | Claims accepted with copied labels | With IDs | Result |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Which companies are developing osimertinib? | 10/12 | 12/12 | sponsorship only; counts preserved |
| What trials evaluate Tagrisso? | 24/24 | 24/24 | EVALUATES + phase/status for each trial |
| What does osimertinib target? | 1/1 | 1/1 | acts on EGFR (inhibitor) |
| Which drugs act on ALK? | 20/22 | 22/22 | ACTS_ON + stage/trial count |
| What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC? | 12/12 | 12/12 | sponsor counts preserved |
| What is the capital of France? | no evidence | no evidence | refusal before calling LLM |
| What is osimertinib? | 2/2 | 2/2 | profile stage/count plus mechanism |

Rendering references: `src/structured.py:243-267`. Sponsor sentence explicitly limits the claim to lead sponsorship, matching the graph derivation's lead_sponsor filter (`src/graph.py:337-356`). No duplicate live EVALUATES trial/drug pairs exist in this snapshot; the edge count equals trial count here.

Exact multi-phase input: question `What trials evaluate BEXAROTENE?`, `{"claims":[{"kind":"trial","trial":"NCT00153842","evidence":"NCT00153842|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1023|NCT00153842","phase":["PHASE1","PHASE2"],"status":"TERMINATED"}]}`.
Observed: `verified=true`, text `- NCT00153842: phase PHASE1, PHASE2; status TERMINATED [NCT00153842|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1023|NCT00153842]`.

There is one live empty-phase trial (`NCT00499330`) but no EVALUATES edge, hence no live default-retrieval accepted empty-phase case. An explicitly marked in-memory fixture changed only NCT03909334 phases to []; accepted phase=[] rendered `no phase given; status ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING`. This is fixture coverage, not a claim about the production snapshot.

Action distinction: with question `What does aprinocarsen target?`, subject `APRINOCARSEN`, object `ENSG00000154229`, and evidence `CHEMBL2108287|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000154229|CHEMBL2108287`, action `ANTISENSE INHIBITOR` accepts and renders `(antisense inhibitor)`; changing only action to `INHIBITOR` rejects with `the graph gives the action as ANTISENSE INHIBITOR, not INHIBITOR`.

### PROFILE is supported data but is not a live graph edge

Question `What is osimertinib?`:
`{"claims":[{"kind":"drug","drug":"OSIMERTINIB","evidence":"profile:CHEMBL3353410","max_stage":"APPROVAL","trials_in_graph":79}]}` accepts and renders `- OSIMERTINIB: maximum clinical stage APPROVAL; 79 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph [profile:CHEMBL3353410]`.
SQL `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE id='profile:CHEMBL3353410' AND retired_at IS NULL` returns `0`.
The special case at `structured.py:113-115` is evidence-consistent because node properties and counts are checked, but `structured.py:26-27` and README.md:452-453 should qualify their literal live-edge wording to mention PROFILE citations.

### Closed schema cannot preserve all evidence metadata

`structured.py:48-55` supports only ACTS_ON, EVALUATES, SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF and trial phase/status, drug max stage/count. It cannot express drug type, alias count, sponsor class, trial title/enrollment, full mechanism descriptions, target/disease associations, parent ownership, or source confidence. Evidence can display some of these (`query.py:251,281,352,358`), but adding `drug_type` or `aliases_known` to a valid profile claim rejects as unknown keys (exact probes in `faithful_results.json`). This is a declared completeness limitation, not false support.

### Documentation inconsistencies (not additional soundness failures)

- `query.py:12-18` still says the model only changes prose and entities/numbers in generated prose are checked; the current model path chooses structured claims and the program renders them.
- `web/template.html:261` still describes a check after answer creation for names/numbers; lines 263-267 correctly describe the new structured model path and template non-applicability.
- README.md:534 says 22 of 38 faithful answers were **rejected** in round 6, while README.ko.md:370 says 22 of 38 **passed**. These cannot both be the same result. This review did not replay the auxiliary checker (explicitly out of scope).
- README.md:458 says no model text reaches the reader, immediately followed by rejected claims being listed; the precise guarantee should refer to the rendered answer. Korean README and try box 3 already use answer-specific wording. Literal rejection display is expected by the brief; no inference of HTML execution from displaying escaped text.
- README item13 and try box4 clearly label the auxiliary prose checker fallible; dashboard section06 and both README structured sections correctly limit completeness/relevance claims.


identity_findings.md
# Independent identity and value review

Commit: `aa5655ae89c47631fefb9c868989ba2c1befa82e`.

Reproduce in PowerShell:

```powershell
$env:PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE='1'
python audit_evidence_claims\s1\identity_probe.py
```

This writes only `identity_probe_results.json` beside the script. The live SQLite connection is query-only. Two explicitly labelled regression fixtures use isolated in-memory database copies and do not describe current persisted graph contents.

## BLOCKERS

### Whole-name guarantee is bypassed by non-ASCII text

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`

Exact payload:

```json
{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"osimertinib가 아닌","object":"EGFR","evidence":"CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
```

Observed: `verified=true`, `rejected=[]`, one accepted relation. `link_entities` reports `matched_text="osimertinib가"`, omitting the final word `아닌`. The full subject means "not osimertinib" rather than naming that drug alone. The rendered text is:

```text
- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor) [CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410]
```

`src/structured.py:64-65` removes all non-ASCII letters before `resolve` compares the matched text and full subject at `src/structured.py:85-92`. This violates the documented whole-name identity condition (`src/structured.py:29`). It does **not** make the canonical rendered graph fact unsupported or leak the discarded subject text. `非osimertinib` and `osimertinib是安全的` also pass. Full evidence: `identity_probe_results.json:297`.

## SHOULD FIX

### Malformed value types raise exceptions instead of a rejected claim

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`

Exact minimal input: `{"claims":[{"kind":[]}]}`. `kind not in KEYS` at `src/structured.py:100` raises `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'`. Replacing `relation` with `[]` in a complete valid relation claim similarly raises at `src/structured.py:122`. Dictionaries in either field behave the same way. The probe uses complete claims for these tests. Output evidence starts at `identity_probe_results.json:1635`.

Exact raw string construction `'{"claims":' + '[' * 5000 + 'null' + ']' * 5000 + '}'` raises `RecursionError: maximum recursion depth exceeded while decoding a JSON array from a unicode string`. `'{"claims":[' + '9' * 5000 + ']}'` raises `ValueError` for the 4,300-digit integer conversion limit. `verify` catches only `JSONDecodeError` (`src/structured.py:210-215`). Output evidence: `identity_probe_results.json:7908` and `:7920`.

### An exact canonical salt name in the ALK demo evidence is rejected

Question: `Which drugs act on ALK?`

Exact payload:

```json
{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE","object":"ALK","evidence":"CHEMBL3707320|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000171094|CHEMBL3707320","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
```

Observed: `verified=false`, `accepted=[]`, rejection `"'ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE' does not name exactly one drug on its own"`. The linker accumulates both the salt and parent drug (`src/query.py:147-170`), so `len(ents)==1` fails (`src/structured.py:86`). Changing only subject to `CHEMBL3707320` succeeds and renders the salt label. This is an expressible graph fact but the names-as-written prompt (`src/query.py:458`) leads to a false rejection. Evidence: `identity_probe_results.json:7932` and `:7979`. Treat as usefulness issue rather than unsupported acceptance.

## NOTES

- The live graph has no multi-id company key and no live edge whose endpoint is absent or retired. All 142 currently retired edges reject even when explicitly added to the retrieved-id allowlist. Normal aliases and exact IDs work; salt-to-parent substitution, foreign-question evidence, wrong relation, unsupported action, wrong counts, and mixed phase types reject.
- Stored code inventories have no `_norm` collision for action types, phases, statuses, or maximum stages. In particular, `ANTISENSE INHIBITOR` does not equal `INHIBITOR`. Optional separator/case variants pass and render the original DB value. Dictionaries and counts are in `identity_probe_results.json:8021` onward.
- `True`, floats, numeric strings, null, arrays, NaN and Infinity reject for integer fields. Duplicate JSON object keys take their final value: a final supported action passes, a final unsupported action rejects. No unchecked earlier value reaches rendered text.
- Fifty-one valid claims yield 50 accepted claims plus a rejection saying the rest were not read; overall `verified=false`. Twenty-thousand-character invalid action rejects; a 1,100-deep list is rejected as a non-object claim on this interpreter, while 5,000 nesting raises as above.
- A PROFILE pseudo-id is not an SQL edge, yet legitimate node-backed profile claims pass. This is evidence-consistent but the literal "live edge" docstring guarantee needs an exception (`src/structured.py:113-115`).
- Synthetic-only fixture: giving `companies['astrazeneca']` both AstraZeneca and Genentech IDs makes `resolve` return two IDs but the AstraZeneca claim passes. `src/structured.py:92,133` tests membership instead of singleton equality. This is a latent mismatch with the exact-one-node guarantee, not a currently reachable multi-company example. Evidence: `identity_probe_results.json:11363`.
- Synthetic-only fixture: marking osimertinib retired in an in-memory DB leaves the resolved dictionary intact; its name-based PROFILE claim still passes. Alias resolution does not recheck live node status and `_drug` fetches props without a live filter (`src/structured.py:85-92,187`). The current graph contains no retired Drug node. Evidence: `identity_probe_results.json:11413`.

+# Independent structured-answer verification
+
+Reviewed HEAD `aa5655ae89c47631fefb9c868989ba2c1befa82e` against the user's A–F brief. No tracked files were edited. New scratch artifacts are confined to `audit_evidence_claims/s1/`. The auxiliary prose checker was not audited. No paid/network LLM call was made: the actual `_llm_claims` and `answer(use_llm=True)` code ran against injected SDK responses. HTTP requests used an ephemeral localhost server; the page test executed the actual JavaScript handler in a Node VM with a minimal DOM, not a full browser.
+
+`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`: **300 passed in 8.57s**, using UTF-8 mode, disabled bytecode and a temporary directory under this audit directory. An earlier run also passed all 300 tests but emitted a Windows cp949 subprocess decoding warning; the UTF-8 rerun removed it. Logs: `root_pytest.txt`, `root_pytest_utf8.txt`.
+
+## BLOCKERS
+
+### B1. Whole-name identity check accepts a partially linked name containing negation
+
+Question: `What does osimertinib target?`
+
+Exact input:
+
+```json
+{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"osimertinib가 아닌","object":"EGFR","evidence":"CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
+```
+
+Observed output: `verified=true`, one accepted claim, `rejected=[]`, and:
+
+```text
+- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor) [CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410]
+```
+
+The full subject means “not osimertinib”; it is not a standalone drug name. `link_entities` reports only `matched_text="osimertinib가"`, omitting `아닌`. Yet `_flat("osimertinib가") == _flat("osimertinib가 아닌") == "osimertinib"`. `src/structured.py:64-65` deletes meaningful non-ASCII letters, and `src/structured.py:85-92` treats this lossy comparison as proof of a whole-name match. Dictionary key normalization also removes these characters (`src/textrules.py:339-346`). Replacing the subject with `非osimertinib` or `osimertinib是安全的` likewise passes.
+
+This contradicts the explicit whole-name acceptance condition in `src/structured.py:29`, `README.md:454`, and `README.ko.md:318`. It is an **accepted-input identity violation**. The resulting canonical graph sentence itself remains supported; this finding does **not** establish unsupported rendered biology, medical free-text leakage, or XSS. The stronger advertised claim-verification contract is the reason approval fails.
+
+Required correction: validate the entire supplied name against a complete entity name/alias using normalization that does not erase semantic letters or unmatched words. Require exactly the cited node identity. Keep the graph-only renderer.
+
+Reproduction: `root_probe.py`, cases `name:'osimertinib가 아닌'`, `name:'非osimertinib'`, `name:'osimertinib是安全的'`; independently reproduced in `identity_probe.py`. Exact raw results: `root_probe_results.json`; `identity_probe_results.json:297`.
+
+## SHOULD FIX
+
+### S1. Canonical evidence names can be rejected even when copied exactly
+
+Question: `Which drugs act on ALK?`
+
+```json
+{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE","object":"ALK","evidence":"CHEMBL3707320|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000171094|CHEMBL3707320","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
+```
+
+Observed: `verified=false`, `accepted=[]`, reason `'ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE' does not name exactly one drug on its own`. Changing only `subject` to `CHEMBL3707320` accepts. The linker collects both the salt and its parent (`src/query.py:147-170`), and `src/structured.py:86` then rejects the exact salt label.
+
+Question: `Which companies are developing osimertinib?`
+
+```json
+{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF","subject":"Second Affiliated Hospital Of Nanchang University","object":"OSIMERTINIB","evidence":"ORG:secondaffiliatedhospitalofnanchanguniversity|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL3353410|","trial_count":2}]}
+```
+
+Observed: `verified=false`, `accepted=[]`, reason `'Second Affiliated Hospital Of Nanchang University' does not name exactly one company on its own`. Its canonical `ORG:secondaffiliatedhospitalofnanchanguniversity` ID accepts. `Tianjin Medical University Cancer Institute And Hospital` has the same failure. The linker only tries five-token windows (`src/query.py:147`). The model prompt explicitly requests names as written in evidence (`src/query.py:456-457`).
+
+Outside the demos, `What does aprinocarsen target?` displays target `PRKCA`, but that symbol rejects; `ENSG00000154229` accepts. The target name index only includes the selected target list (`src/query.py:101-102`). Full payloads and outputs: `faithful_results.json`, `faithful_additional.json`, `faithful_findings.md`.
+
+These are usefulness defects. ID-based faithful claims remain expressible and accepted, so they are not additional false-support blockers.
+
+### S2. Malformed kinds, relations and extreme JSON escape as exceptions
+
+For `What does osimertinib target?`, the exact reply `{"claims":[{"kind":[]}]}` causes `answer(use_llm=True)` to raise `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'`. A dictionary instead of the list gives `unhashable type: 'dict'`. Replacing `relation` in an otherwise valid relation claim with `[]` does the same. Cause: membership tests precede string-type checks (`src/structured.py:100,122`). HTTP catches the exception and returns 400 `{"error":"unhashable type: 'list'"}` (`src/app.py:137-139`), but direct/model callers lose the result, including earlier valid claims.
+
+Exact raw string expression `'{"claims":' + '[' * 5000 + 'null' + ']' * 5000 + '}'` raises `RecursionError`. `'{"claims":[' + '9' * 5000 + ']}'` raises the integer conversion limit `ValueError`. `src/structured.py:210-215` catches only `JSONDecodeError`. Recorded in `identity_probe_results.json:7908,7920`; the model harness also reproduces nesting at 20,000 levels. These failures accept no unsupported claim.
+
+### S3. NaN is rejected but reflected into an invalid JSON API response
+
+Question: `What does osimertinib target?`; exact inner `answer` string:
+
+```text
+{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"osimertinib","object":"EGFR","evidence":"CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410","action":NaN}]}
+```
+
+Observed: verifier rejects with `an action belongs to an ACTS_ON claim, as a string`. `/api/structured` returns HTTP 200 containing literal `"action": NaN` inside the rejected claim. The page displays `오류: Unexpected token 'N', ...""action": NaN}, "why"... is not valid JSON` instead of the rejection. Python's permissive load at `src/structured.py:212` and dump at `src/app.py:135` disagree with the browser's parser at `web/try.html:173`. Infinity also rejects as an action. Reject non-finite JSON constants before reflecting them. Exact wire data: `modelui_api_fixtures.json`, case `nan_action`; HTML: `modelui_page_results.json`.
+
+### S4. Empty or malformed SDK response blocks are unchecked
+
+Actual `_llm_claims` with injected SDK output `content=[]` raises `IndexError: list index out of range`; `content=[SimpleNamespace(type='tool_use')]` raises `AttributeError: 'types.SimpleNamespace' object has no attribute 'text'`; `content=[SimpleNamespace(text=None)]` raises `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'strip'`. A simulated SDK exception propagates. Cause: `src/query.py:460-464`. Ordinary empty text and malformed JSON do return a closed rejection. See `modelui_results.json` cases `no_content`, `non_text_block`, `null_text`, `sdk_failure`.
+
+## NOTES
+
+### N1. All demo facts supported by the schema can be accepted with IDs
+
+Exact questions, payloads, retrieved evidence and complete rendered answers are in `faithful_results.json`. Each trial produces an EVALUATES claim and a phase/status claim; each ALK drug produces relation and stage/count claims.
+
+| Question | Claims accepted using displayed labels | Using exact IDs |
+|---|---:|---:|
+| Which companies are developing osimertinib? | 10/12 | 12/12 |
+| What trials evaluate Tagrisso? | 24/24 | 24/24 |
+| What does osimertinib target? | 1/1 | 1/1 |
+| Which drugs act on ALK? | 20/22 | 22/22 |
+| What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC? | 12/12 | 12/12 |
+| What is the capital of France? | refusal, no evidence | refusal, no evidence |
+| What is osimertinib? | 2/2 | 2/2 |
+
+For example, question `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?`, claim `{"kind":"relation","relation":"SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF","subject":"AstraZeneca","object":"osimertinib","evidence":"ORG:astrazeneca|SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF|CHEMBL3353410|","trial_count":14}` accepts and renders `Astrazeneca is lead sponsor of 14 trial(s) evaluating OSIMERTINIB - a sponsorship, not evidence of developing the drug`, followed by its edge citation. This matches graph derivation from lead sponsorship (`src/graph.py:337-356`, renderer `src/structured.py:249-252`).
+
+For `What trials evaluate BEXAROTENE?`, claim `{"kind":"trial","trial":"NCT00153842","evidence":"NCT00153842|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1023|NCT00153842","phase":["PHASE1","PHASE2"],"status":"TERMINATED"}` accepts and renders `NCT00153842: phase PHASE1, PHASE2; status TERMINATED`, with that citation. Empty phases render `no phase given` in an explicitly labelled in-memory fixture; the only current live empty-phase trial has no EVALUATES edge, so no current retrieved example was asserted. See `faithful_empty_phase_fixture.json`.
+
+### N2. Rendering and rejected-claim HTML did not leak executable model content
+
+Use the B1 relation with ordinary `subject="osimertinib"` and add `note='<img src=x onerror="globalThis.MODEL_INJECTED=1"><script>globalThis.MODEL_INJECTED=2</script>&"\''`. It rejects as unknown `note`; using the same string as `action` rejects. `text` is empty. The actual page handler produces `&lt;img` and `&lt;script` in the diagnostic display, without raw injected elements. All answer/rejection/evidence/error text passes `esc` (`web/try.html:93,176-181`).
+
+The same HTML string surrounding a valid model JSON object is removed by extraction, and the answer is the canonical OSIMERTINIB sentence. `Answer.checks` and the page intentionally retain rejected model text as diagnostics, not as verified answer text. Therefore README.md:458's literal “nothing the model typed reaches the reader” should say “rendered answer”; its next sentence already says rejected claims are listed. Test limitations: Node VM handler/escaping test, not full browser execution. Exact payloads/HTML: `modelui_results.json`, `modelui_page_results.json`.
+
+### N3. Edge/value adversarial controls fail closed; normalization has no current code collisions
+
+`identity_probe_results.json` contains 128 live-graph cases plus all 142 retired-edge checks. All retired edges reject even when deliberately included in the evidence allowlist. Wrong-direction/relation claims, evidence from a different question, salt-to-parent substitutions, ambiguous drug aliases and mismatched values reject. Exact IDs and valid aliases accept. Current action-type, phase, status and stage dictionaries have no `_norm` collisions; inventories are preserved in that file.
+
+Concrete action contrast: for `What does aprinocarsen target?`, `{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"APRINOCARSEN","object":"ENSG00000154229","evidence":"CHEMBL2108287|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000154229|CHEMBL2108287","action":"ANTISENSE INHIBITOR"}` accepts and renders `(antisense inhibitor)`; changing only action to `INHIBITOR` rejects: `the graph gives the action as ANTISENSE INHIBITOR, not INHIBITOR`. Booleans, floats, numeric strings, null, lists and non-finite values reject for integer count fields.
+
+### N4. Parsing and count-limit behavior is conservative, with reporting quirks
+
+`src/query.py:465` greedily extracts the first `{` through last `}`. A valid object in a code fence accepts. Concatenating that object with `{"claims":[]}` rejects with invalid JSON; text outside the braces is not rendered. Duplicate `action` keys are last-wins: `"action":"ACTIVATOR","action":"INHIBITOR"` accepts the checked inhibitor; reversing the two rejects. This is not strict duplicate-key rejection but does not render the discarded unsupported value.
+
+Input `{"claims":[C]*51}` (Python construction, where C is the ordinary valid OSIMERTINIB relation) accepts 50, returns `verified=false`, and adds `{"claim":null,"why":"more than 50 claims; the rest were not read"}` (`src/structured.py:223-230`). A large unknown-field model payload rejects; the API truncates its request read to 20,000 bytes and returns HTTP 400 on the resulting malformed JSON.
+
+The prompt asks for `{"claims":[]}` when no evidence answers the question (`src/query.py:457-458`), but `src/structured.py:217-220` treats it as an invalid envelope. For a supported question the result is zero accepted, one rejection, and `None of the model's claims could be verified.\n\n(1 claim(s) were removed because the graph does not support them.)`. This misleadingly counts a removed claim where none was supplied. Missing SDK follows the same route with an error in `generated_by`. A mix of valid and rejected claims yields graph-only answer text but `checks.grounded=false`: this flag describes the submitted payload, not unsupported text surviving rendering. Cases and exact outputs: `modelui_results.json`; mixed-payload behavior is also covered by the passing test at `tests/test_structured.py:106-115`.
+
+### N5. PROFILE and other documentation qualifications
+
+Question `What is osimertinib?`, payload `{"claims":[{"kind":"drug","drug":"OSIMERTINIB","evidence":"profile:CHEMBL3353410","max_stage":"APPROVAL","trials_in_graph":79}]}` accepts and renders `- OSIMERTINIB: maximum clinical stage APPROVAL; 79 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph [profile:CHEMBL3353410]`. SQL `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE id='profile:CHEMBL3353410' AND retired_at IS NULL` returns `0`. `src/structured.py:113-115` creates this pseudo-edge; properties and counts are checked against the graph. Evidence-consistent, but the literal live-edge wording in `src/structured.py:26-27` and `README.md:452-453` needs this exception.
+
+The schema rejects a profile claim extended with `"drug_type":"Small molecule"` or `"aliases_known":9` as unknown keys. It also has no kinds for sponsor class, trial title/enrollment, parent ownership, disease associations, source confidence or full mechanism prose (`src/structured.py:48-55`). These are completeness limitations, not false acceptance.
+
+`src/query.py:12-18` still says the model only changes prose; `web/template.html:261` still describes a post-prose names/numbers check. The implementation and following dashboard paragraph instead use structured selection and program rendering. `README.md:534` says 22/38 faithful answers were rejected in round 6, while `README.ko.md:370` says 22/38 passed. Those statements conflict; the out-of-scope prose checker was not rerun to decide which historical number is correct. README item 13 and try page box 4 correctly describe the auxiliary checker as fallible. The structured sections correctly disclaim relevance/completeness apart from B1 and the qualifications above.
+
+### N6. Two fixture-only future-state risks, not current-graph counterexamples
+
+The current company index has no multi-ID key. In a deliberately altered in-memory index, setting `companies['astrazeneca']=['ORG:astrazeneca','ORG:genentech']` and submitting the ordinary AstraZeneca/OSIMERTINIB/14 claim still accepts. `resolve` returns both IDs (`src/structured.py:92`), and `_relation` only tests membership (`:133`), not singleton equality. This is a latent second mismatch with the exact-one-identity contract.
+
+In an isolated in-memory SQLite copy, marking `CHEMBL3353410` retired while retaining its dictionary alias still permits `{"kind":"drug","drug":"osimertinib","evidence":"profile:CHEMBL3353410","max_stage":"APPROVAL"}`. Alias lookup and PROFILE node reads do not recheck node liveness (`src/structured.py:85-92,188`). The current persisted graph has no retired Drug node and no live edge with a retired/missing endpoint. Exact fixture setup and results: `identity_probe.py`, `identity_probe_results.json:11363,11413`.
+

+"""Independent structured-answer adversarial checks; never writes the source graph."""
+import copy
+import hashlib
+import json
+import sqlite3
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / 'src'))
+import query
+from structured import Verifier
+
+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+kg = query.OncoKG()
+kg.conn.execute('PRAGMA query_only=ON')
+q = 'What does osimertinib target?'
+entities = kg.link_entities(q)
+ev = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(q, entities), entities)
+base = {'kind': 'relation', 'relation': 'ACTS_ON', 'subject': 'osimertinib',
+        'object': 'EGFR', 'evidence': ev[0].edge_id, 'action': 'INHIBITOR'}
+results = []
+
+def probe(tag, payload, evidence=ev):
+    row = {'case': tag, 'question': q, 'input': payload}
+    try:
+        row['output'] = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
+    except Exception as exc:
+        row['exception'] = {'type': type(exc).__name__, 'message': str(exc)}
+    results.append(row)
+
+probe('baseline', {'claims': [base]})
+for name in [' osimertinib ', 'OSIMERTINIB', 'osimertinib is safe',
+             'osimertinib\u662f\u5b89\u5168\u7684', '\u975eosimertinib',
+             'osimertinib\uac00 \uc544\ub2cc', 'osimertinib\u200b',
+             'osimertinib / placebo', 'osimertinib,osimertinib',
+             'osimertinib\x00', 'osimertinib' + ' ' * 120,
+             'o' * 121, 'ＯＳＩＭＥＲＴＩＮＩＢ']:
+    c = dict(base, subject=name)
+    probe('name:' + repr(name), {'claims': [c]})
+values = [None, True, False, 0, 1, 1.0, '1', '', [], {}, ['ACTS_ON'],
+          ['INHIBITOR', 1], float('nan'), float('inf')]
+for key in base:
+    for value in values:
+        probe('type:' + key + ':' + repr(value), {'claims': [dict(base, **{key: value})]})
+for key in ['note', '<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>', 'text']:
+    probe('unknown_key:' + key, {'claims': [dict(base, **{key: 'untrusted'})]})
+for count in [0, 1, 50, 51, 1000]:
+    probe('claim_count:' + str(count), {'claims': [base] * count})
+for raw in ['{"claims": [', 'null', 'NaN', 'Infinity', '{}', '[]',
+            '{"claims": [' + json.dumps(base) + '], "claims": []}',
+            '{"claims": [], "claims": [' + json.dumps(base) + ']}',
+            '{"claims": [' + json.dumps(base)[:-1] + ', "action":"ACTIVATOR"}]}',
+            '{"claims": [' + json.dumps(base)[:-1] + ', "action":"INHIBITOR"}]}',
+            '[' * 1100 + '0' + ']' * 1100]:
+    probe('raw_json:' + raw[:80], raw)
+
+manifest = {'commit': 'aa5655ae89c47631fefb9c868989ba2c1befa82e',

Reviewed `aa5655a`. **300 tests passed. No tracked files changed.** Full reproductions, outputs, and A–F coverage are in [REPORT.md](<C:/Users/geoum/Dropbox/GY's Formal Workspace/2026 Job recruting/바이오리서치에이아이/oncokg/audit_evidence_claims/s1/REPORT.md>).

**BLOCKERS**

**B1 — The whole-name identity guarantee fails.**

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`

Exact input:

```json
{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"osimertinib가 아닌","object":"EGFR","evidence":"CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
```

Observed: `verified=true`, one accepted claim, `rejected=[]`.

```text
- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor) [CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410]
```

The subject means “not osimertinib.” The linker matches only `"osimertinib가"`, omitting `"아닌"`. Nevertheless, `_flat` deletes the Korean letters from both strings, making the partial match appear complete.

Cause: `src/structured.py:64–65,85–92`; violated guarantees: `src/structured.py:29`, `README.md:454`, `README.ko.md:318`.

**This is an accepted-input identity failure. The rendered graph fact remains supported; this does not demonstrate unsupported medical prose or HTML leakage.** Approval fails against the explicitly promised whole-name condition.

**SHOULD FIX**

1. **Faithful names copied from evidence reject.** For `Which drugs act on ALK?`:

   ```json
   {"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE","object":"ALK","evidence":"CHEMBL3707320|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000171094|CHEMBL3707320","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
   ```

   Output: `verified=false`, `accepted=[]`, reason:
   `'ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE' does not name exactly one drug on its own`.

   Changing only the subject to `CHEMBL3707320` accepts. The linker collects salt and parent together (`src/query.py:147–170`; `src/structured.py:86`). Two long sponsor labels also reject because linking stops at five tokens. Exact sponsor inputs are in the report.

2. **Malformed types throw instead of returning rejection.** For the osimertinib question, input `{"claims":[{"kind":[]}]}` raises `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'` through `answer(use_llm=True)`. Array/dictionary `relation` values behave similarly (`src/structured.py:100,122`). HTTP catches this as 400, but direct/model callers receive no result.

3. **Rejected NaN breaks the page’s JSON response.** Use B1’s payload with ordinary subject `"osimertinib"` and replace action with the unquoted token `NaN`. The verifier correctly rejects it, but `/api/structured` returns HTTP 200 containing `"action": NaN`. The page displays `Unexpected token 'N' ... is not valid JSON`. See `src/structured.py:212`, `src/app.py:135`, `web/try.html:173`.

4. **Response-shape and parsing failures escape.** Mocked SDK response `content=[]` raises `IndexError` at `src/query.py:464`. Exact input expression `'{"claims":' + '['*5000 + 'null' + ']'*5000 + '}'` raises `RecursionError`; only `JSONDecodeError` is caught (`src/structured.py:210–215`). These fail closed.

**NOTES**

- **Usefulness:** All **73** supported demo/profile claims accept using exact IDs. Copied labels accept 69/73. Trial phase/status, sponsor counts, drug stages/counts, and supported relations are expressible. France correctly receives a refusal.
- **Rendering and HTML:** Adding `note:"<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>"` to an otherwise valid claim rejects as an unknown key; answer text is empty and diagnostic HTML contains `&lt;img`. No tested model text entered the rendered answer. Page verification executed the actual JavaScript handler in a Node VM, not a full browser (`web/try.html:176–181`).
- **Values and edges:** No current stored-code `_norm` collisions were found. `ANTISENSE INHIBITOR` accepts for aprinocarsen; substituting `INHIBITOR` rejects. All **142 retired edges** reject, including with stale allowlist entries.
- **Parsing:** Fifty-one valid claims produce 50 accepted plus an excess-count rejection. Duplicate action keys use the final value, which is checked. Fenced JSON accepts; concatenated JSON objects reject.
- **Empty replies:** `{"claims":[]}` produces one rejection and says one claim was removed, although the prompt explicitly requests this empty response (`src/query.py:457–458`; `src/structured.py:217–220`).
- **PROFILE documentation:** For `What is osimertinib?`, a drug claim citing `profile:CHEMBL3353410`, stage `APPROVAL`, and count `79` accepts. SQL finds zero corresponding live edges. Its facts remain graph-supported, but the “live edge” wording needs a PROFILE exception (`src/structured.py:113–115`).
- **Other documentation:** `src/query.py:12–18` and `web/template.html:261` retain obsolete prose-check descriptions. English README says 22/38 faithful answers were rejected; Korean README says 22/38 passed. Schema omissions and relevance/completeness limitations remain NOTES.

**BLOCKERS**

**B1 — The whole-name identity guarantee fails.**

Question: `What does osimertinib target?`

Exact input:

```json
{"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"osimertinib가 아닌","object":"EGFR","evidence":"CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
```

Observed: `verified=true`, one accepted claim, `rejected=[]`.

```text
- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor) [CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410]
```

The subject means “not osimertinib.” The linker matches only `"osimertinib가"`, omitting `"아닌"`. Nevertheless, `_flat` deletes the Korean letters from both strings, making the partial match appear complete.

Cause: `src/structured.py:64–65,85–92`; violated guarantees: `src/structured.py:29`, `README.md:454`, `README.ko.md:318`.

**This is an accepted-input identity failure. The rendered graph fact remains supported; this does not demonstrate unsupported medical prose or HTML leakage.** Approval fails against the explicitly promised whole-name condition.

**SHOULD FIX**

1. **Faithful names copied from evidence reject.** For `Which drugs act on ALK?`:

   ```json
   {"claims":[{"kind":"relation","relation":"ACTS_ON","subject":"ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE","object":"ALK","evidence":"CHEMBL3707320|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000171094|CHEMBL3707320","action":"INHIBITOR"}]}
   ```

   Output: `verified=false`, `accepted=[]`, reason:
   `'ALECTINIB HYDROCHLORIDE' does not name exactly one drug on its own`.

   Changing only the subject to `CHEMBL3707320` accepts. The linker collects salt and parent together (`src/query.py:147–170`; `src/structured.py:86`). Two long sponsor labels also reject because linking stops at five tokens. Exact sponsor inputs are in the report.

2. **Malformed types throw instead of returning rejection.** For the osimertinib question, input `{"claims":[{"kind":[]}]}` raises `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'` through `answer(use_llm=True)`. Array/dictionary `relation` values behave similarly (`src/structured.py:100,122`). HTTP catches this as 400, but direct/model callers receive no result.

3. **Rejected NaN breaks the page’s JSON response.** Use B1’s payload with ordinary subject `"osimertinib"` and replace action with the unquoted token `NaN`. The verifier correctly rejects it, but `/api/structured` returns HTTP 200 containing `"action": NaN`. The page displays `Unexpected token 'N' ... is not valid JSON`. See `src/structured.py:212`, `src/app.py:135`, `web/try.html:173`.

4. **Response-shape and parsing failures escape.** Mocked SDK response `content=[]` raises `IndexError` at `src/query.py:464`. Exact input expression `'{"claims":' + '['*5000 + 'null' + ']'*5000 + '}'` raises `RecursionError`; only `JSONDecodeError` is caught (`src/structured.py:210–215`). These fail closed.
